# Build State

(Last updated: 2026-05-06 — Brief 43 completed; GM/RM/admin/super_admin can now flip a claim's `equipment_related` no→yes when approving into the two active-repair branches ("Approved — Pending Quotes", "Approved — In House — Parts Ordered"). New apps/web `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` client island (`apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx`) renders a modal-gated submit button on those two transition rows when `claim.equipment_related === 0`; modal asks "Was this damage equipment related?" with No/Yes radios + a 6-option `equipment_piece` dropdown (hard-forked from worker `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES`); confirm appends `override_equipment_related` + `override_equipment_piece` hidden inputs to the existing transition `<form>` and re-submits via `form.requestSubmit()` so the existing `<ActionForm>` + `useActionState` pipeline handles the dispatch unchanged. Worker `handleStatusTransition` now reads/validates the override fields (yes only valid on the two target statuses, only no→yes flips, equipment_piece required + ≤200), folds `equipment_related = 1` + `equipment_piece = ?` into the same UPDATE that applies the status change, then post-batch fires `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` — re-reads `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` and short-circuits when non-null (defense-in-depth dedupe), constructs a transient post-UPDATE `ClaimRow`, calls Brief 42's `createMaintainXWorkOrder` with the same fail-soft posture (8 s `AbortController` timeout, helper never throws, `[maintainx]` activity-log entries on success/failure). Worker response now optionally includes `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` flags; `transitionAction` surfaces a "Status updated to X, but the MaintainX work order couldn't be created — see activity log" success-toned message on the failure path (the transition itself committed). Override side-effect annotated on the same `status_change` activity row via a `[Equipment override]` sentinel suffix in the notes — no new activity_type introduced (D1 CHECK constraint still blocks them). Two paths now create MaintainX WOs: Brief 42's customer-form submit and Brief 43's GM-side approval override; both share the helper + the `claims.maintainx_workorder_id IS NULL` dedupe gate so re-triggers land at most one WO per claim. Empirical MaintainX probe still deferred to operator from Brief 42; Brief 43 inherits the same unverified-response-shape posture.)

## Status snapshot

The 5-worker port + 7 shared packages are feature-complete on `pnpm typecheck` (13/13 green) and deployed only to `*.workers.dev`; apps/web has 8 real pages + 0 placeholders + 3 Step-4 stubs, with Brief 1 (login + middleware), Brief 2 (legacy URL redirects + global Header + logout flow), Brief 4 (admin landing dashboard), Brief 5a (damage claim list), Brief 5b (damage claim detail, read-only), Brief 5c (damage write actions: transitions, notes, check-request preview), Brief 5d (damage documents: upload, edit, delete + photo lightbox), Brief 6 (performance tracker list + new-submission form + LocationPicker typeahead), and Brief 7 (sysadmin UI: five-card super_admin gate consuming all 5 sysadmin-worker mutation endpoints) landed — the damage manager UI, performance tracker UI, and sysadmin UI are at functional parity with legacy. Production routes for the new workers remain commented in every `wrangler.toml`, and the legacy `info-signup-worker` still owns customer signup traffic on `splashcarwashes.info`. Cutover is gated on apps/web reaching parity for admin / signup / claim surfaces and on a dedicated cutover-strategy plan being run for the only production-critical worker (signup).

| 37 | Brief 37 | Mobile quote-upload fix — bypass Next 15 server actions for the multipart upload path. Legacy `info-signup-worker` upload (`legacy/damagemanager.js:2446` `handleDocumentUpload`) "just worked" on iPhone Safari because it's a vanilla CF-Worker multipart POST + 303 redirect; the new monorepo port routed the same multipart through `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` (a Next 15 server action invoked through OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers), which threw the digest 924441341@e394 white-page on iPhone (Brief 36 Part B). Brief 37 retires the server-action path entirely: `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` was rewritten as a plain `<form action="/manage/api/claim/{id}/document" method="POST" enctype="multipart/form-data">` — relative URL resolves through CF same-zone routing in prod/staging and through `next.config.mjs`'s `/manage/api/:path*` rewrite in dev. `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` `handleDocumentUpload` was changed to 303-redirect on every branch (success → `${appsWebOrigin}/admin/damage/{claimId}`; validation/storage failure → same path with `?upload_error=<msg>`; new helper `buildUploadRedirect(request, claimId, errorMessage?)`); the apps-web origin is read from the request's `Origin` header (set by the browser on every form POST), falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin` for the same-zone production case. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` deleted (along with its `damagePostMultipart` import and Brief 36 Part B's defensive try/catch — both now dead code). `damagePostMultipart` deleted from `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (sole consumer was the deleted action). `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` reads `?upload_error=...` searchParam and renders a new `UploadErrorBanner` above `UploadDocumentCard` when present (re-introduces the param-based error pattern Brief 19 retired, scoped strictly to this one form because it bypasses `<ActionForm>`). The "Add document" anchor button (`<a href="#upload-document">`) was added next to the existing "Add note" button in `RecentNotesBox` — same shape, same smooth-scroll target (the global `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }` from Brief 22 still applies). HEIC handling unchanged: `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` (HEIC → JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough on conversion failure). **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` (handleDocumentUpload + new buildUploadRedirect helper), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` (full rewrite — drop ActionForm, plain form posting to `/manage/api/...`), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (delete uploadDocumentAction + drop damagePostMultipart import; re-title docblock), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (delete damagePostMultipart), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` (read ?upload_error + render UploadErrorBanner; add "Add document" anchor button in RecentNotesBox), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 37 row marked Completed), `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` (Brief 37 line moved to completed-tombstone block), `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-037-mobile-upload-legacy-port-plus-add-doc-anchor.md` (Outcome filled in, Status set to Completed). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none (logical deletions of `uploadDocumentAction` and `damagePostMultipart` are line-level edits inside surviving files). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker returns 303 unconditionally** — every error branch redirects with `?upload_error=<msg>` rather than returning JSON. The brief explicitly chose Option A ("Worker responds with a 302 to /admin/damage/{claim_id} — browser follows the redirect"); JSON return values would only matter for non-browser API consumers, and the only consumer was the deleted `uploadDocumentAction`. (2) **Form action is a relative URL** (`/manage/api/claim/{id}/document`) rather than an absolute env-resolved URL — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, works in dev via the existing `next.config.mjs` rewrite. Avoids passing a server-resolved URL prop into the client component, which would have re-introduced the apps/web↔worker indirection this brief is designed to eliminate. (3) **Apps-web origin for the redirect Location is read from the `Origin` header**, falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin`. Browser sets `Origin` on every POST submission; same-zone production has matching origin/url-origin so both paths land at the same place; cross-zone dev (without Next rewrites) gets the apps/web localhost origin via the header. (4) **`?upload_error=<msg>` re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired**, scoped to this one form. Brief 19's pattern flip was specifically for Next 15 server actions whose `redirect()` calls were unreliable on OpenNext-on-CF-Workers; raw HTTP form POSTs from a plain `<form>` to a Cloudflare Worker have no such issue (browser handles the redirect natively). The banner reads `?upload_error` once at SSR; on a fresh load (no form submit) the param is absent and nothing renders. (5) **`damagePostMultipart` deleted entirely** rather than left in place for future use — sole consumer was `uploadDocumentAction`, both retired together. CLAUDE.md guidance: "If you are certain that something is unused, you can delete it completely." (6) **No new pattern abstraction** — brief explicitly out-of-scoped a generalized "raw worker form" helper. The single use case is the document upload; if a second one shows up later, lift the pattern then. (7) **HEIC handling left as-is** — `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2`. Brief Phase 2.4 said "If the legacy code transcodes HEIC to JPEG before the R2 write, replicate that. … otherwise the legacy worker probably just passes HEIC through" — verified the new helper already transcodes via `env.IMAGES` when bound, falls back to passthrough when not, which is identical to legacy. No code change needed. (8) **`UploadErrorBanner` is rendered above `UploadDocumentCard`** (between the photo gallery and the upload form) rather than at the top of the page next to the confirm-delete banner. The error is contextually about the upload action; rendering it where the user's eye goes after the failed submit is the smaller cognitive jump. (9) **`docType`/`payToType` client state preserved** in the rewritten card so the conditional `required` UX (Brief 20 Bugs 5/6) still gates Quote-row amount/pay_to/vendor/vendor_address — worker re-validates as defense in depth, but pre-submit native HTML `required` attrs are still the better UX. (10) **The "Add document" anchor button's HTML structure mirrors "Add note" exactly** — same container, same Tailwind classes, same `<a href>` shape; the only diff is the href target (`#upload-document` vs `#add-note`) and label text. Pattern continuity matters more than micro-optimizing the markup. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-origin dev without Next rewrites is unsupported.** If the operator runs `next dev` without `NEXT_PUBLIC_DAMAGE_WORKER_URL` set, the upload form's relative-URL POST 404s on apps/web (no rewrite to fall through to). Pre-existing limitation per `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts`'s docblock; this brief inherits the same posture. (b) **The submit-then-redirect pattern is page-reload-based, not optimistic.** Operators see a brief blank-then-loaded transition between submit and the post-redirect SSR. Compared to the prior `<ActionForm>` flow's `useActionState` spinner + inline result banner, this is slightly less polished; brief explicitly chose this trade-off (matches legacy iPhone path that took 2 seconds end-to-end). (c) **`?upload_error=<msg>` persists in URL after the user dismisses by clicking elsewhere.** A hard-reload re-shows the banner. Future polish could clear the param via `router.replace()` from a small client island wrapping the banner, but this isn't worth a separate brief unless operators complain. (d) **No mobile smoke test executed in headless** — the brief's DoD includes "Smoke test: upload an HEIC photo from iPhone Safari to staging — should succeed in <5 seconds." Per CLAUDE.md headless constraint, this session does not deploy; operator runs the smoke test on next deploy. (e) **Bundle delta on damage-worker:** 1661.70 KiB → 1662.94 KiB uncompressed (+1.24 KiB) / 376.97 KiB → 377.28 KiB gzip (+0.31 KiB). Slightly above the brief's "+0.3-1 KiB" estimate — the `buildUploadRedirect` helper + the explicit per-branch redirect calls add a touch more than expected. Comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. (f) **Bundle delta on apps/web:** post-Brief-37 `/admin/damage/[id]` is 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS (Next build output). The apps/web client-bundle delta is dominated by removing `<ActionForm>`'s `useActionState`/`useEffect` plumbing from the upload card; net: down a small number of bytes vs. the pre-Brief-37 baseline. Server-side action count drops from 5 to 4 in the route. (g) **Brief 36 Part B's defense-in-depth try/catch on `uploadDocumentAction` is now retired alongside the action itself** — the underlying root cause (server-action multipart path) is excised, so the wrap is no longer load-bearing. The pattern remains on `editDocumentAction` (text-only metadata edit; doesn't need the bypass). (h) **`uploadDocumentAction` is gone from `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts`'s exports** — any future code that imports it gets a TypeScript error pointing at the change. Grep across `apps/web` confirms zero remaining imports post-deletion. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.517s (11 cached, 2 cache-miss — `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` ran fresh as expected). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 7.9s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=./dist` succeeded — bundle 1662.94 KiB / 377.28 KiB gzip (+1.24 KiB / +0.31 KiB vs Brief 35 baseline). **Operator action items:** (i) on next staging deploy, smoke-test HEIC upload from iPhone Safari — should succeed in <5 seconds (matches the legacy 2-second baseline that motivated this brief); (ii) verify the `?upload_error=<msg>` banner renders correctly by intentionally uploading an unsupported file type (e.g., `.txt` or oversized 11 MB JPEG); (iii) verify the "Add document" anchor button at the top of the claim detail page smooth-scrolls to the upload card on mobile + desktop. | **completed** (2026-05-06) | mobile upload bug closed; legacy port complete | items 5d (upload pipeline this brief patches), 22 (anchor-button + smooth-scroll pattern), 36 (try/catch defense-in-depth landed in Part B; this brief addresses underlying cause) |

| 43 | Brief 43 | GM/RM/admin/super_admin equipment-related override modal on the two active-repair approval transitions ("Approved — Pending Quotes" and "Approved — In House — Parts Ordered"). **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` — single named export `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` (client island that renders the transition form's submit button + a modal-gated pre-flight). Modal asks "Was this damage equipment related?", segmented No/Yes radios with No defaulted, reveals an `equipment_piece` `<select required>` on Yes (6 options hard-forked from `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES` at `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts:18` — Top Brush / Side Wraps / Conveyor / Dryer / Wheel Blaster / Other; comment in the new file pins the worker as canonical so future edits coordinate both sides). Click handler intercepts the submit only when `modalEnabled && enabled` (otherwise the form submits normally), opens the modal, and on confirm appends `override_equipment_related` ("yes"/"no") + `override_equipment_piece` (when yes) hidden inputs to the parent `<form>` and calls `form.requestSubmit()` — re-uses the existing `<ActionForm>` + React 19 `useActionState` pipeline unchanged. ESC + click-outside both cancel without submitting. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — `handleStatusTransition` now reads `override_equipment_related` + `override_equipment_piece` from the form, validates (yes-only on the two target statuses, only no→yes flips, equipment_piece required + ≤200 chars; "no" or absent passes through unchanged), folds `equipment_related = 1` + `equipment_piece = ?` into the same UPDATE that lands the status change, then post-batch fires a new module-local `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` helper. The helper re-reads `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` via `getClaimById` (defense-in-depth dedupe — the loaded claim row's snapshot is pre-update, and a concurrent retry could have landed a WO meanwhile), constructs a transient post-UPDATE `ClaimRow` with `equipment_related=1`, `equipment_piece=overrideEquipmentPiece`, `claim_status=finalTo`, calls Brief 42's `createMaintainXWorkOrder` with the same fail-soft posture (8 s `AbortController` timeout, helper never throws past the handler), writes a `[maintainx]` `note`-typed activity-log entry on success/failure (success: "Work order #N created via approval-time override (mode: M)"; failure: "Override WO creation failed — {error} (status: S, mode: M)"). The status_change activity row's notes field gets a `[Equipment override] {actor} flipped equipment_related to yes during "{finalTo}" approval (equipment_piece: P)` sentinel suffix on the same status_change row — no new activity_type introduced. The transition response now includes optional `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` flags (only when the override fired); apps/web `transitionAction` reads them and surfaces "Status updated to X, but the MaintainX work order couldn't be created — see activity log" on the failure path so the operator knows to check the timeline. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — `TransitionSection` + `TransitionForm` now accept `equipmentRelated: 0 | 1`; new module-local `EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS` set holds the two target statuses; the inline `<button type="submit">` was swapped for `<EquipmentOverrideSubmit>` with `modalEnabled={equipmentRelated === 0 && EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS.has(to)}`. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` — `transitionAction` reads `result.body` and emits the failure-toast message when `maintainx_attempted === true && maintainx_ok === false`. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Equipment-piece options hard-forked into the modal file** rather than hoisted into `@splash/types` — the brief explicitly delegated this to executor judgment. The list is short (6 entries), rarely changes, and the in-file comment pins the worker constant as canonical so future edits coordinate both sides. The cross-package alternative would have required a runtime export from `@splash/types` (which today is types-only) plus a re-export through both `@splash/damage-worker` (server) and `apps/web` (client) — three touch points instead of two. (2) **Modal-show condition is purely client-side**: the worker re-validates as defense-in-depth (rejecting yes-with-other-target and yes-when-already-yes), but the apps/web modal only opens when `claim.equipment_related === 0` AND target is one of the two active-repair statuses AND the button is enabled. Hidden inputs are appended via `appendHiddenInput` which dedupes by name — re-opening the modal after a previous confirm updates in place rather than appending duplicates. (3) **`form.requestSubmit(submitButton)` invoked with the button as the submitter** so the form's submit pipeline picks up the same React `formAction` it would on a native click — the `<ActionForm>` `useActionState` handler reacts to it identically. (4) **Post-batch dedupe re-read** in `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` short-circuits when `maintainx_workorder_id != null` already — covers the scenario where Brief 42's form-submit flow created a WO but the GM somehow approved with override anyway (shouldn't happen because the modal's `modalEnabled` check gates on the loaded `equipment_related === 0`, but a stale page state + concurrent submission could in theory race). The first writer wins via `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId`'s "UPDATE … WHERE maintainx_workorder_id IS NULL" semantics; the dedupe re-read avoids the wasted MaintainX POST. (5) **Override side-effect on the same status_change row** rather than a separate activity row — per brief Phase 1.5 ("don't introduce a separate activity_type"). The `[Equipment override]` sentinel + `[maintainx]` follow-up rows on the timeline tell the full story; the existing `note`-typed timeline entries cover the audit need. (6) **WO URL pattern + empirical probe** still inherited from Brief 42 — Brief 43 doesn't introduce new MaintainX call shape; the helper signature was stable before this brief landed. (7) **Modal styling uses inline tailwind classes** rather than ModalShell from `@splash/ui` — `ModalShell` is a 5-modal overlay primitive sized for the signup-worker's success/deny/warn cards, with hard-coded card width (max 420px, padded `40px 30px`, centered text). The override modal's content (left-aligned copy, segmented choice, conditional dropdown, two-column action row) didn't fit cleanly inside ModalShell's prescribed layout. The ad-hoc dialog implementation matches the visual posture of the existing confirm-delete banner pattern on the same page. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Empirical MaintainX probe still pending** — inherited from Brief 42; flipping `MAINTAINX_MODE` to `"production"` is still gated on the operator running the probe and confirming the WO ID extractor's fallback chain works. (b) **The override modal's "Yes" branch fires the MaintainX hook synchronously inside the request lifecycle** — adds up to 8 seconds (the AbortController timeout) to the transition's response time on the WO-failure path. apps/web's `<ActionForm>` shows the loading state via `data-pending="true"`; operators on a slow MaintainX response will see the button disabled for the duration. Acceptable for v1; future polish could move the hook to `ctx.waitUntil()` (out of scope per CLAUDE.md "no abstractions beyond what the task requires"). (c) **Worker's response-shape extension only emits `maintainx_attempted`/`maintainx_ok` on the override path** — non-override transitions return the same shape they always did, so existing apps/web message handling is unchanged for every other transition. (d) **The "Cancel" button + outside click + ESC all close the modal without firing the transition** — the underlying form is unchanged in DOM, so a subsequent click of the same submit button re-opens the modal in its initial state (No selected, no piece). (e) **No headless smoke test possible** — the brief asked to confirm "smoke-testing both paths in test mode produced exactly one MaintainX WO per claim (no duplicates from the dedupe gate)"; this requires a live MaintainX call which headless cannot exercise. Operator must run the smoke test post-deploy: (i) submit a customer claim with `equipment_related=1` (Brief 42 path), confirm one WO; (ii) on a separate claim, submit with `equipment_related=0`, then GM-approve into one of the two target statuses with the modal saying Yes, confirm one WO; (iii) attempt to submit the override modal twice on the same claim and confirm the dedupe gate prevents a second WO. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (7.119s, cache hit on 11, fresh build on `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` as expected — only those two packages were modified). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 9.3s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle **3.1 kB → 4.11 kB / 108 kB → 109 kB First Load JS (+1.01 kB / +1 kB)** — `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` client island accounts for the delta. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded — bundle **1679.53 KiB → 1685.55 KiB uncompressed (+6.02 KiB) / 380.73 KiB → 381.86 KiB gzip (+1.13 KiB)** vs. Brief 42 baseline; comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. The three new `[vars]` entries from Brief 42 still appear in the dry-run binding table. **Operator action items:** (1) Run the smoke test described in latent-issues (e). (2) The PA Parse JSON schema doesn't need updating — the override path doesn't extend the `claimData` payload (the WO ID lives on the claim row in D1, not on the transition's PA hook). (3) If the modal copy needs tweaking ("Was this damage equipment related?" / "...assigned to maintenance.") it lives in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` as plain JSX. | **completed** (2026-05-06) | Nothing | items 42 (`createMaintainXWorkOrder` helper + `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` dedupe key this brief reuses), 41 (`damage_type` allow-list — read by the WO title via Brief 42's `buildTitle`), 19 (`<ActionForm>` server-action pattern this brief works inside), 5c (transition handler + UI surface) |
| 42 | Brief 42 | MaintainX work order auto-creation on `equipment_related=1`. **Phase 1 — D1 schema:** `ALTER TABLE claims ADD COLUMN maintainx_workorder_id INTEGER` run REMOTE on `splash-damage-claims` D1; PRAGMA confirms cid 56 (INTEGER, nullable). **Phase 2 — wrangler.toml + Env:** new top-level `[vars]` block in `apps/damage-worker/wrangler.toml` (`MAINTAINX_MODE = "test"`, `MAINTAINX_BASE_URL = "https://api.getmaintainx.com/v1"`, `APPS_WEB_BASE_URL = "https://splashcarwashes.info"`); `Env` interface in `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` extended with `MAINTAINX_API_KEY?: string` (secret, optional — fail-soft skip when unbound), `MAINTAINX_MODE: "production" \| "test"`, `MAINTAINX_BASE_URL: string`, `APPS_WEB_BASE_URL: string`. **Phase 3 — helper module:** new `apps/damage-worker/src/maintainx.ts` exports `createMaintainXWorkOrder(input)` and `MaintainXResult`. Body shape: `{ title, description, priority: "HIGH", categories: ["Vehicle Damage"], assignees, locationId? }` (locationId omitted when null — MaintainX accepts WOs without a location). Helper never throws: fetch errors / non-2xx / non-JSON / missing WO id all return `{ ok: false, ... }`. WO ID extracted via fallback chain (`body.id` → `body.workOrder.id` → `body.data.id`) so the empirical-probe-pending response shape doesn't block the brief. **Phase 4 — handleClaimSubmission wiring:** hook fires inside the writeClaimBatch try block, AFTER location_pretty resolution and BEFORE the Power Automate POST so the WO ID can ride along on the PA payload. Constructs a transient `ClaimRow` from `insert` (avoids an extra D1 round trip) and calls the helper with an 8000ms `AbortController` timeout. On success: `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId(env.DB, claim_id, workOrderId)` writes the column AND the activity log gets a `[maintainx] Work order #{id} created (mode: {mode})` `note`-typed row. `claimData.maintainxWorkorderId` is set so the in-memory PA payload includes it (R2 submission JSON saved at step 4 already; doesn't pick up the late-arriving field — acceptable since PA is the authoritative downstream consumer). On failure: activity log gets `[maintainx] Work order creation failed — {error} (status: {status}, mode: {mode})`. Customer-facing 303 → /thanks redirect path is unchanged. **Phase 5 — Supabase helper:** new `getMaintainXLocationId(env, locationCode)` in `packages/db-supabase/src/locations.ts` does a TWO-STEP lookup because `locations` does NOT have a `location_code` column (sysadmin-worker confirms this at `index.ts:682`): step 1 fetches `pricing_simple.site` filtered by `location_code=eq.<sanitized>` (limit 1, `select=site`), step 2 fetches `locations.maintainx_id` filtered by `site=eq.<site>` (limit 1). If the field is `body.workOrder.id` or `body.data.id`, the helper already handles it; if it's something else, edit `extractWorkOrderId` in `apps/damage-worker/src/maintainx.ts`. (2) **Verify the WO URL pattern** — `app.getmaintainx.com/workorders/{id}` is plausible but unverified. Brief 43's GM modal will also use the UPDATE path (the row already exists). Adding it to ClaimInsert would be no-op churn. (e) **Test WO created during probing: zero from this session** — headless cannot exercise live MaintainX. PostgREST has no SQL `DISTINCT` projection, so the worker fetches up to 200 raw rows ordered by `site.asc` and dedupes server-side by `location_code` (first occurrence wins), capping the response at 50 distinct entries. Empty/whitespace-only `q` → `[]`. **Phase 2 — apps/web client island:** new `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/LocationCodePicker.tsx` mirrors the UserPicker contract — props `{ name, inputId, required?, initialValue? }`, 250 ms debounced fetch via `setTimeout` + `fetchSeqRef` race-guard, hidden `<input type="hidden" name={name}>` carrying the canonical `location_code`, combobox/listbox aria roles + arrow nav + Esc + outside-click dismiss + `aria-activedescendant`. Each dropdown row renders `#{site} {location_pretty} {location_code}` with the canonical code in mono font for visual distinction. **Phase 3 — wire into Set Role card:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx` swapped the `<input name="location_code">` for `<LocationCodePicker name="location_code" inputId="set-role-location-code" />`; `FieldLabel` text changed from "Location code" to "Location" with helper "Required for location_admin role — search by site #, name, or code"; help text below the picker preserved verbatim; `setRoleAction` unchanged (the picker writes to the same `location_code` form field name the action already reads). **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx`. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/LocationCodePicker.tsx`. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Visible input is replaced by the selection chip rather than sitting alongside it** — UserPicker shows both (input + chip below) because the input is the primary search affordance and the chip is informational. For LocationCodePicker the chip *is* the resolved selection; leaving an empty input below it would be misleading (typing more would replace the resolved value). The "× Clear" link in the chip restores the input. Cosmetic-only divergence from the UserPicker exact spec. (2) **`FieldLabel` relabeled "Location code" → "Location"** — the visible affordance is no longer a code-typed input. Hidden form field name is still `location_code` (worker contract unchanged). (3) **Server-side dedupe rather than client-side** — brief specified DISTINCT location-level fields as the response shape; doing it server-side keeps the client component thin and the response contract clean. The 200-row raw fetch / 50-row deduped cap matches the brief's example. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Create User card has the same problem and is intentionally out of scope** — `brief-040-create-user-location-code-picker.md` is already queued to fold the same picker into that card (file at line 106-117 of `_sections/UserOperations.tsx`). No new follow-up needed. (b) **Operator should run `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy` from the appropriate session to ship the new endpoint** — per CLAUDE.md headless constraint, this session does not deploy. The picker depends on the new endpoint, so a missed worker deploy would leave the picker fetching a 404 from the old worker bundle (the picker would render an inline "Search failed (404)" red note under the input but the card would otherwise function — empty dropdown, no crash). **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 6.045s (11 cached, 2 cache-miss — `@splash/web` and `@splash/sysadmin-worker` ran fresh as expected); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 6.7s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/sysadmin` bundle 7.06 kB → 8.14 kB / 112 kB → 113 kB First Load JS (+1.08 kB / +1 kB, in the brief's "+1-2 kB" predicted range). sysadmin-worker has no `build` script per the existing pattern (`typecheck` is the build verification). **Operator action items:** (i) on next sysadmin-worker deploy, smoke-test the picker — open `/admin/sysadmin?mode=users`, click "Set role", type `111` / `Binghamton` / `batavia_v` and verify the dropdown returns one row per matching location; pick a row and verify the chip shows `#{site} {location_pretty}` with the canonical `location_code` in mono; submit with role=location_admin and confirm `set_role` audit log row records the picked code. (ii) Brief 40 (Create User picker fold-in) is already queued. | Claude Code |

| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 37 completed:** Mobile quote-upload bug closed by retiring the Next 15 server-action path for the multipart upload. **Root cause confirmed via legacy code read:** `legacy/damagemanager.js:2446` `handleDocumentUpload` is a vanilla CF-Worker handler that calls `request.formData()`, validates fields, calls the existing `uploadToR2(file, claimId, photoType, seq, env)` helper (line 260 — HEIC→JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough on conversion failure), inserts the `claim_photos` row, then `Response.redirect(\`${url.origin}/manage/claim/${claimId}\`, 303)` (line 2620). The shape "browser POST → worker → 303 → SSR detail page" is exactly what worked on iPhone Safari in the 2026-05-05 operator test (~2 seconds end-to-end). The new monorepo port had the same handler shape on the worker side (apps/damage-worker uses the same `uploadClaimPhoto` helper from `@splash/storage-r2`, which mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` line-for-line including HEIC handling) BUT routed the multipart through `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` — a Next 15 server action invoked through the OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers runtime. That extra hop reframed the multipart body into a server-action FormData payload and re-encoded the file across an apps/web → service-binding → damage-worker subrequest, which is what produced the digest 924441341@e394 white-page on iPhone (Brief 36 Part B). **Fix:** apps/web's `UploadDocumentCard` was rewritten as a plain `<form action="/manage/api/claim/{id}/document" method="POST" enctype="multipart/form-data">` that POSTs directly to the damage-worker's existing endpoint (relative URL — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, in dev via the existing `/manage/api/:path*` rewrite in `next.config.mjs` when `NEXT_PUBLIC_DAMAGE_WORKER_URL` is set). The damage-worker's `handleDocumentUpload` was changed to 303-redirect on every branch (success → `${appsWebOrigin}/admin/damage/{claimId}`; validation/storage failure → same path with `?upload_error=<msg>`) — new helper `buildUploadRedirect(request, claimId, errorMessage?)` reads the apps-web origin from the request's `Origin` header (set by the browser on every form POST), falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin` for the same-zone production case. The apps/web side now reads `?upload_error=...` searchParam in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` and renders an `UploadErrorBanner` above the upload card; this re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired, scoped strictly to this one form (Brief 19's pattern flip was for Next-server-action `redirect()` calls, which are unreliable on OpenNext-on-CF-Workers — raw HTTP form POSTs from a plain `<form>` to a Cloudflare Worker have no such issue, browser handles the redirect natively). The deleted-on-retirement code: `uploadDocumentAction` in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (with its Brief 36 Part B defensive try/catch — both no longer load-bearing) and `damagePostMultipart` in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (sole consumer was the deleted action). **Phase 3 anchor button:** added `<a href="#upload-document">Add document</a>` next to the existing `<a href="#add-note">Add note</a>` button in `RecentNotesBox`. Same Tailwind classes, same shape — only the href target and label differ. The smooth-scroll CSS rule (`html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }`) was already in place from Brief 22; `id="upload-document"` was already on `UploadDocumentCard`'s root from Brief 20. **HEIC handling:** unchanged. `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` line-for-line (HEIC→JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough). Verified during Phase 1 read; no code change needed. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` (handleDocumentUpload + new buildUploadRedirect helper), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` (full rewrite — drop ActionForm, plain form posting to relative URL), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (delete uploadDocumentAction + drop damagePostMultipart import + retitle docblock), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (delete damagePostMultipart), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` (read ?upload_error + render UploadErrorBanner; add "Add document" anchor button in RecentNotesBox), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 37 row marked Completed), `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` (Brief 37 line moved to completed-tombstone block), `BUILD_STATE.md` (this entry + Last updated bump + prioritized work list row 37), `BRIEFS/brief-037-mobile-upload-legacy-port-plus-add-doc-anchor.md` (Outcome filled in, Status set to Completed). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none (logical deletions of `uploadDocumentAction` and `damagePostMultipart` are line-level edits inside surviving files; the source files themselves stay). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker returns 303 unconditionally** — every error branch redirects with `?upload_error=<msg>` rather than returning JSON. The brief explicitly chose Option A; JSON return values would only matter for non-browser API consumers, and the only consumer was the deleted `uploadDocumentAction`. (2) **Form action is a relative URL** rather than absolute env-resolved — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, works in dev via `next.config.mjs` rewrite. Avoids passing a server-resolved URL prop into the client component. (3) **Apps-web origin for redirect Location is read from Origin header**, falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin`. (4) **`?upload_error=<msg>` re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired**, scoped to this one form because it bypasses `<ActionForm>` entirely. (5) **`damagePostMultipart` deleted entirely** — sole consumer was `uploadDocumentAction`; both retired together per "If you are certain that something is unused, you can delete it completely." (6) **No new pattern abstraction** — brief explicitly out-of-scoped a generalized "raw worker form" helper. (7) **HEIC handling left as-is** — verified `uploadClaimPhoto` already matches legacy. (8) **`UploadErrorBanner` rendered above `UploadDocumentCard`** rather than at top of page — error is contextually about the upload action. (9) **`docType`/`payToType` client state preserved** — Brief 20's conditional `required` UX still gates Quote-row fields. (10) **"Add document" button structure mirrors "Add note" exactly** — pattern continuity. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-origin dev without Next rewrites is unsupported** — pre-existing limitation, no regression. (b) **The submit-then-redirect pattern is page-reload-based, not optimistic** — slightly less polished than the prior `<ActionForm>` flow's spinner + inline result banner; brief explicitly chose this trade-off (matches legacy iPhone path that took 2 seconds). (c) **`?upload_error=<msg>` persists in URL after the user dismisses by clicking elsewhere** — a hard-reload re-shows the banner. Future polish could `router.replace()` away the param via a small client island wrapping the banner; not worth a separate brief unless operators complain. (d) **No mobile smoke test executed in headless** — operator runs the smoke test on next deploy. (e) **Bundle delta on damage-worker:** 1661.70 KiB → 1662.94 KiB uncompressed (+1.24 KiB) / 376.97 KiB → 377.28 KiB gzip (+0.31 KiB). Slightly above the brief's "+0.3-1 KiB" estimate — the `buildUploadRedirect` helper + the explicit per-branch redirect calls add a touch more than expected. Comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. (f) **Bundle delta on apps/web:** post-Brief-37 `/admin/damage/[id]` is 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. Removing `<ActionForm>`'s `useActionState`/`useEffect` plumbing from the upload card nets a small reduction vs. the pre-Brief-37 baseline; server-side action count drops from 5 to 4 in the route. (g) **Brief 36 Part B's defense-in-depth try/catch on `uploadDocumentAction` is now retired alongside the action itself** — root cause excised, wrap no longer load-bearing. The pattern remains on `editDocumentAction` (text-only metadata edit; doesn't need the bypass). (h) **No remaining `uploadDocumentAction` consumers** — grep across `apps/web` confirms zero imports post-deletion. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.517s (11 cached, 2 cache-miss — `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` ran fresh as expected). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 7.9s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=./dist` succeeded — bundle 1662.94 KiB / 377.28 KiB gzip. **Operator action items:** (i) on next staging deploy, smoke-test HEIC upload from iPhone Safari — should succeed in <5 seconds; (ii) verify `?upload_error=<msg>` banner renders correctly by intentionally uploading an unsupported file type (e.g., `.txt` or oversized 11 MB JPEG); (iii) verify the "Add document" anchor button at the top of the claim detail page smooth-scrolls to the upload card on mobile + desktop. | Claude Code (Brief 37 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 36 completed:** Test batch — three independent items grouped by the 2026-05-05 staging test session. **Part A (PDF humanize labels) — landed as specified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-summary-pdf.ts` gained a `humanizeLabel(v: string \| null \| undefined): string` helper next to the existing `dash()` helper. Splits on `[_-]`, drops empty parts (defense against double-underscore tokens like `a__b`), title-cases each remaining part (first-letter uppercase, rest lowercase), joins with a single space. Null/undefined/whitespace-only → `"—"` to match `dash()`'s null-rendering. Wired into Staff Assessment in two places: `equipmentRelated` (replaced the inline `=== "yes" / === "no"` ternary with one `humanizeLabel(...)` call for one consistent path) and `determination` (replaced `value || "—"` with `humanizeLabel(value)`). Smoke-trace of the brief's enumerated determination values: `caused_by_car_wash` → "Caused By Car Wash", `not_caused_by_car_wash` → "Not Caused By Car Wash", `partial_responsibility` → "Partial Responsibility", `no_responsibility` → "No Responsibility", `under_review` → "Under Review", `yes`/`no` → "Yes"/"No", null → "—". The `dash()` helper still owns Customer Information rendering (plain "show value or em-dash"); humanization is strictly the enum-shaped path per the brief's A.3. **Part B (mobile quote upload digest `924441341@e394`) — defense-in-depth fix landed; root-cause investigation deferred:** the brief's B.1 step ("find the unscrubbed error message keyed by digest in CF dashboard logs") requires Cloudflare dashboard access, which headless Claude Code does not have. The unscrubbed exception text is **NOT** documented in this Outcome — operator action item is to open Cloudflare dashboard → Workers & Pages → splash-web → Logs → search digest `924441341@e394` (Real-time Logs while a tester reproduces, or Logpush archive if enabled) and forward the unscrubbed message + stack trace; the targeted patch (per the brief's likely-culprits list — multipart parse / HEIC / oversized photo) lands in a follow-up brief. **What did land** is the defense-in-depth try/catch wrap mandated by the brief's B.3 constraints: `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` `uploadDocumentAction` body wrapped in `try { … } catch (err) { console.error(...); return { ok: false, error: \`Document upload failed: \${msg}\` }; }`. Mirrors the Brief 20 Bug 7 pattern already on `editDocumentAction`. Why this is a real fix even without root-cause knowledge: in Next 15 / OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers, an uncaught throw inside a server action escapes the action and fails the surrounding server-component render boundary — that render-boundary failure is exactly the user-visible symptom (the production build scrubs the message and emits the digest white-page). The wrap converts every throw into an inline `<ActionForm>` red banner — page stays mounted, operator sees the actual underlying error message (not a digest), and the next session can identify the root cause from the inline banner text rather than CF logs alone. **Part C (Update Package multi-select rewrite) — landed as specified:** **Worker (`apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts`):** new `PATCH /sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/packages-bulk` route — added to `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS`, dispatched in the PATCH switch, audit-action `update_packages_bulk` added to `ALLOWED_AUDIT_ACTIONS`. New `handleUpdatePackagesBulk` (~120 LOC) validates `location_code` against `LOCATION_CODE_RE`, validates `updates` array length 1..20 (`BULK_MAX_UPDATES = 20`), per-entry: requires non-empty `pkg`, rejects `BULK_REJECTED_FIELDS` (Brief 26's denorm columns + the scoped-out `pkg_new`/`flash2`/`flash5`/`pricing`/`location_pretty`), validates `pkg$` non-negative non-null, `single`/`sort` non-negative-or-null with positive-int check on `sort`, dedupes pkg names within the request, requires at least one editable field per entry. Per-row PATCH loop issues one Supabase REST PATCH per entry with `Prefer: return=representation`; partial failures don't abort peers; response shape `{ ok: true, location_code, results: [{pkg, ok, error?}], updated, failed }` for the UI's per-row breakdown. One audit log entry per request (`action: "update_packages_bulk"`, `target_id: location_code`, `after: { location_code, updates, updated, failed }`) — per-row entries would spam the log per the brief's spec. Existing `handleUpdatePackage` (Brief 26 single-row) gets a deprecation note in its docblock per §C.3; handler stays routed for SQL/curl fallback consumers. **apps/web actions (`apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts`):** new `updatePackagesBulkAction` server action reads `bulk_pkg_<i>_selected` keys to discover selected indices, then `bulk_pkg_<i>_pkg` (travels), `bulk_pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` (required, non-negative), `bulk_pkg_<i>_single` (empty → null), `bulk_pkg_<i>_sort` (empty → null; positive int when present). Caps at `BULK_PACKAGES_MAX_SELECTED = 20` (matches worker). Builds `UpdatePackagesBulkBody`, PATCHes via existing `sysadminPatchJson`, surfaces worker's per-row results in the success message: zero-failure → "<n> packages updated at <code>"; partial-failure → "<u> updated, <f> failed: <pkg names>" still ok-status (per brief: "Even with partial failure, return 200 with ok: true"). **apps/web client islands:** `PackageSearchPicker.tsx` repurposed from per-row composite-PK search to one-option-per-location dedupe — hits the existing `GET /sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search?q=...` endpoint and dedupes via Map keyed by `location_code` (no new endpoint added; brief preferred reuse to a thinner `/pricing-simple/locations` endpoint). New exported types `PricingSimpleSearchRow` (kept for the package-list consumer) + `BulkLocationOption` (the deduped option shape). `UpdatePackageCard.tsx` full rewrite — 4-step flow: (1) `<PackageSearchPicker>` location pick; (2) `useEffect` fetches all packages at the chosen `location_code` via the same search endpoint, filters strictly to `r.location_code === code` (defends against substring cross-location matches), sorts by `(sort ASC, pkg ASC)`; (3) renders a table with header "select all" checkbox + per-row checkboxes; checked rows reveal `<input type="number">` for pkg$/single/sort pre-filled with current values, unchecked rows show read-only labels; (4) submit fires `updatePackagesBulkAction` via `<ActionForm>`. Submit button is `disabled` when 0 or >20 rows selected. On success, the card flips `location` identity to refetch the post-save state. **page integration:** `_sections/TableOperations.tsx` card title changed `"Update package"` → `"Update package pricing"` and description rewritten to describe the multi-select scope; `_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` `ALLOWED_ACTIONS` extended with `update_packages_bulk` so the new audit-action surfaces in the activity-log filter dropdown. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-summary-pdf.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts`, `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/PackageSearchPicker.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/UpdatePackageCard.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/TableOperations.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx`, `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-036-test-batch-pdf-humanize-mobile-upload-multi-pkg.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`. **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Part B's targeted root-cause patch is deferred, not guessed at.** Per CLAUDE.md's "When in doubt: ask the operator before guessing. The cost of a clarification question is small; the cost of building the wrong thing is large." and the brief's B.4 ("Outcome must include: The unscrubbed error message + stack trace from CF logs"), this session does not pick from the likely-culprits list (multipart parse / HEIC / oversized photo / etc.) without the actual exception in hand. The defense-in-depth fix is independent of root cause and the brief explicitly mandated it as a constraint regardless. (2) **Reused `/pricing-simple/search` for location dedupe** rather than adding a new `/pricing-simple/locations` endpoint — brief explicitly preferred this order; client-side dedupe of ≤50 returned rows is microsecond-cost. (3) **`pkg$` is REQUIRED on every selected row, not nullable** — both worker and action enforce this. `pricing_simple.pkg$` is NOT NULL on the DB; making it nullable would surprise the operator more than help. (4) **Substring filter strictness** — card filters fetched packages to `r.location_code === code` exactly because the worker's `ilike` substring search can match across locations when the code substring-appears elsewhere. (5) **20-row cap matches worker exactly** — both surfaces use 20; UI disables submit when over. (6) **Brief 26's single-row endpoint stays alive but deprecated** per §C.3. (7) **Per-row failures don't roll back peers** per brief's partial-success spec. (9) **Sort key for the package list is `(sort ASC, pkg ASC)`** — mirrors the worker-side sort on Brief 26's search. | 2026-05-05 | **Brief 32 completed:** customer claim form closes the loop. (A) **Email required.** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` — email input gains `required` + new hint "We'll email you a copy of this claim." `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` `handleClaimSubmission` — worker-side trim + regex validation (`^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$`); on miss returns `{ ok: false, error: "Email required" }` with status 400 (or 303 redirect to `/claims/<slug>?error=Email%20required` in browser mode). D1 column stays nullable for back-compat with pre-Brief-32 rows. (B) **Claim summary PDF.** New module `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-summary-pdf.ts` (~15 KiB, ~390 lines) exporting `generateClaimSummaryPdf(input: ClaimSummaryPdfInput) -> Uint8Array`. Pure programmatic pdf-lib layout (no AcroForm template; no operator pre-work beyond logo upload). US Letter (612×792 pt), 54 pt margins, Helvetica + Helvetica-Bold via StandardFonts. Layout: navy header band (70 pt tall, full-width #1e3a8a) with embedded logo on the left + "Vehicle Issue Report" title + subtitle "claim_id  •  formatted timestamp" right-aligned at 70% white opacity; LOCATION label + pretty/(#code); "Customer Information" section heading (splash-blue rule + bold label) + 5-pair key/value grid (Name / Email / Phone / Vehicle / License Plate); full-width "What Happened" text block (multi-line wrap, 11 pt); optional "Photos" row up to 4 thumbnails (max 120 pt × 90 pt) + `+N more` overflow caption; "Staff Assessment" section heading + 2-pair grid (Staff Name / Equipment-Related) + full-width "Determination" + "What the Customer Was Told" blocks; footer with claim ID + contact note. Internal helpers: `wrapText` (multi-line wrap at content width), `truncateToWidth` (ellipsis-clamping for grid cells), `embedImageBytes` (sniffs JPEG/PNG by header; falls back to attempting both), `Layout` class (cursor + page-break: overflow auto-adds new page, no type shrink). `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` extended: `Env` interface gains `CUSTOMER_CLAIM_WEBHOOK_URL?: string`; new constants `SUMMARY_LOGO_R2_KEY = "assets/splash-logo-white.png"` and `CUSTOMER_WEBHOOK_BASE64_MAX_BYTES = 3 * 1024 * 1024`; new public dispatch `GET /claims-api/summary/{claimId}` → `handleServeClaimSummary` reads `claims/<claimId>/summary.pdf` from R2 and streams `application/pdf` back with `Content-Disposition: inline; filename="claim-<id>.pdf"` and 24 h public cache (mirrors photo-serving posture). New step 8 in the submit pipeline (after PA fail-soft step 7) wraps `buildAndStoreClaimSummaryPdf(env, claimData, baseOrigin)` in try/catch — helper loads logo bytes (R2 first via `SUMMARY_LOGO_R2_KEY`, HTTPS fallback to `ASSETS.logoWhite`, empty-bytes fallback if both fail), pulls up to 4 photo thumbnails directly from R2 at original byte size (no resize — `ImagesBinding`'s minimal interface in `@splash/storage-r2` only supports format conversion, not transform; oversized PDFs are the brief's documented degraded fallback), builds the typed `ClaimSummaryPdfInput`, calls `generateClaimSummaryPdf`, writes bytes to `claims/<claimId>/summary.pdf` with `customMetadata: { claimId, generatedAt }`. (C) **Customer-email webhook.** On PDF success and when `CUSTOMER_CLAIM_WEBHOOK_URL` is bound, `fireCustomerClaimWebhook` POSTs JSON: `claim_id`, `submitted_at`, `location_pretty`, `location_code`, `customer_name`, `customer_email`, `customer_phone`, `vehicle` (assembled "year make model - color"), `summary_pdf_url`, plus `summary_pdf_base64` when raw PDF ≤ 3 MB (≈ 4 MB encoded ceiling). Failures logged + swallowed; submission still returns ok. Unbound env var → silent skip (no log noise; treated as feature-off). `apps/damage-worker/wrangler.toml` commented-secrets block extended to document the new env var + the operator prerequisite for uploading the brand logo PNG. (D) **Form-side polish.** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` outcome card gained `#outcomeDownloadRow` containing `<a id="outcomeDownloadLink" class="btn-primary" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Download a copy (PDF)</a>` — shown only when `summary_pdf_url` is present in the success response. `FORM_SCRIPT.showOutcome` extended to two-arg `(claimId, summaryPdfUrl)`; the fetch handler passes `out.body.summary_pdf_url` through. Worker success response shape extended from `{ ok, claim_id, ... }` to `{ ok, claim_id, summary_pdf_url?, ... }` — programmatic JSON callers get the URL as an optional field; absent on fail-soft skips. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) Photos embed at original byte size, no resize (the `ImagesBinding` interface in `@splash/storage-r2` doesn't expose a resize API; bumping it would touch the package + every consumer). The base64 cap in the customer-email webhook handles oversize gracefully. (2) Logo loaded R2-first (`assets/splash-logo-white.png`) with HTTPS fallback to `ASSETS.logoWhite`; absent both → PDF renders with text-only header band. (3) Dedicated GET route `/claims-api/summary/<id>` rather than reusing `/claims-api/photo/<id>/summary.pdf` — cleaner Content-Disposition + isolates auth-posture if the photo route ever gets gated. (4) No D1 schema change; `customer_email` stays nullable for back-compat. (5) `equipmentRelated` derivation in PDF input: empty/"N/A" → "no", anything else → "yes" (matches existing `equipment_related` 0/1 derivation). (6) Email-validation error shape: 400 JSON / 303 browser redirect with `?error=Email%20required` (mirrors existing handleClaimSubmission error flow). (7) No `claim_photos` row of `photo_type='Summary'` — PDF lives in R2 only; the manager-side document gallery doesn't surface it (out of scope per "Manager-side editing of the PDF"). (8) Fail-soft cascade: PA webhook → R2-fail fallback → PDF gen → customer-email webhook. PDF is strictly additive; submission stays ok regardless. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Logo upload is a soft prerequisite.** Until the operator uploads `assets/splash-logo-white.png` to the `damagedocs` R2 bucket, every submission HTTPS-fetches `ASSETS.logoWhite` (~50-200 ms latency hit). Functional but slow. (b) **Photo bytes are embedded raw — no compression / resize.** Four 4 MB iPhone JPEGs produce a ~16 MB PDF. The webhook base64 cap (3 MB raw → ~4 MB encoded) skips the attachment for any oversized PDF; PA falls back to URL fetch. Forward fix: extend `ImagesBinding` interface for resize. (c) **`licenseState` in PDF input is always null** — form doesn't collect plate state today. PDF render handles null gracefully (License Plate cell shows just the plate number). (d) **No retention policy** on `claims/*/summary.pdf` objects. R2 lives indefinitely until operator decides on a lifecycle rule. (e) **No "regenerate summary PDF" path** — manager-side determination/what-told edits via the damage detail page don't regenerate the customer-facing PDF (frozen at submission-time). Future-brief candidate. (f) **Photos uploaded to R2 BEFORE email-validation gate** — a customer who supplies invalid email and abandons leaves orphaned R2 photo objects.
