| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 31 completed:** Server-action ID stability across deploys + graceful stale-tab UX — fixes the `UnrecognizedActionError` white-page client-exception the operator hit on 2026-05-05 while granting the claims tool to `batavaiawash@splashcarwashes.com`. Two layers landed. **Layer 1 — root cause: stable build-time encryption key.** `apps/web/.env.example` extended with a `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` block documenting (a) why this matters (Next 15 hashes server-action IDs from action-body + key; without a stable key, every `next build` regenerates a fresh random key, invalidating all action IDs in every open tab; submits from pre-redeploy tabs trigger `UnrecognizedActionError` which propagates as the white-page client exception), (b) generation snippets for PowerShell (`RandomNumberGenerator.GetBytes` → `ToBase64String`) + bash (`openssl rand -base64 32`), (c) the build-time-vs-runtime-secret distinction (set in CF dashboard → Workers & Pages → splash-web → Settings → Build → Environment variables; `wrangler secret put` is the WRONG channel because Next reads the env at `next build` time, not at runtime). `apps/web/next.config.mjs` gained a top-of-file comment block flagging `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` as a build-time env var consumed implicitly by Next. **No code change beyond docs** — Next 15.5.15 honors the env var natively. **Operator action item:** generate the 32-byte base64 key once (PowerShell snippet from .env.example), store it in the password manager, set as a build-time env var on splash-web's CF Workers Builds config. The next deploy will produce deterministic action IDs from then on; subsequent deploys keep them stable until the key is rotated. **Layer 2 — defense in depth: segment-level error boundary.** New file `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx` (`"use client"` Next.js error boundary) covering every `/admin/*` route: `/admin/sysadmin`, `/admin/damage/[id]`, `/admin/pricing/*`, and any future action-driven page. Detects `UnrecognizedActionError` via two-token message-substring check (`"Server Action"` AND `"was not found on the server"`) and renders an "App was updated / Reload" CTA bound to `window.location.reload()`; other errors fall through to a generic "Something went wrong / Try again" CTA bound to `reset()`. Includes `console.error` for the full error and `console.warn` of the parsed stale action ID (extracted from `Server Action "<id>" was not found...`) for forward debugging. **CLAUDE.md updates:** new Critical-constraints item 7 ("Never rotate `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` without coordination") with the rotation-during-maintenance-window guidance; new Working-with-apps/web bullet documenting both layers (the env var + the boundary's role + the placement rationale: covers all `/admin/*` action-driven pages from one boundary, don't move it to nested segments). **Files modified:** `apps/web/.env.example` (38 lines added — new section), `apps/web/next.config.mjs` (8-line comment block at file top), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 31 row marked Completed), `CLAUDE.md` (1 new Critical-constraints item + 1 new apps/web bullet), `BUILD_STATE.md` (Last-updated bump, this entry, prioritized work list row 31). **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx` (~85 lines). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Boundary placed at `/admin/error.tsx`, not `/admin/sysadmin/error.tsx` or per-page boundaries** — every server-action write surface (sysadmin, damage detail, future action-driven pages) lives under `/admin/*`, so a single boundary covers them in one shot per the brief's guidance. (2) **Two-token substring check (`"Server Action"` + `"was not found on the server"`) instead of `instanceof` check** — the `UnrecognizedActionError` class isn't exported from any public Next.js entry point, so message-substring is the only stable detection path. The two-token check minimizes false positives over a single-token check. (3) **`console.warn` with the stale ID alongside `console.error` of the full error** — sufficient telemetry hook per the brief's optional B.5; no beacon or external tracking added. (4) **No `global-error.tsx` added** — the segment-level boundary at `/admin/error.tsx` is sufficient for the action-error path because action errors throw inside the segment's render tree (`<ActionForm>` lives under `/admin/*`). If staging testing surfaces a case where the action error escapes past the segment boundary, follow-up brief can add `apps/web/app/global-error.tsx` per B.2's contingency. (5) **`window.location.reload()` for the Reload CTA** rather than `router.refresh()` — `router.refresh()` re-fetches RSC data but keeps the same client bundle in memory, so it would re-render with the same stale action IDs and re-trigger the error on next submit. A full reload picks up the new bundle. (6) **`<button type="button">` without `type` defaulting to submit** — the boundary isn't inside a `<form>` but the explicit `type="button"` prevents any future-form-wrapping accidents. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator-side action item is the gating step.** Until the operator generates the key + sets it as a build-time env var on CF Workers Builds, the next deploy will still produce non-deterministic IDs and Layer 1's protection doesn't activate. Layer 2 (the boundary) provides immediate UX softening regardless. (b) **Existing tabs opened before the key is first set will still hit the boundary on next form submit after the FIRST post-key-set deploy** — those tabs were rendered with the pre-key build's action IDs, which the post-key build doesn't recognize. The boundary catches it gracefully ("Reload" CTA) — operator hits Reload once, picks up the new build, action IDs become stable from then on. (c) **Layer 2 also catches non-action errors** (any uncaught exception thrown from server actions or client components in `/admin/*`). Generic "Something went wrong / Try again" UI is the right shape for those — `reset()` re-renders the segment, which usually clears transient errors. Per brief B.4, `reset()` won't recover from a stale-action error (the same ID would resubmit), so the action-specific path stays on the Reload CTA. (d) **No `global-error.tsx` means uncaught errors OUTSIDE `/admin/*`** (e.g., from `/login`, `/`, `/signup/[location]`) still hit Next's bare client-exception fallback. Those routes don't host server actions and have minimal client logic, so the practical exposure is small. Add a global boundary if/when a non-admin route grows action-driven write surfaces. (e) **Bundle delta unobservable.** `/admin/*` route First Load JS unchanged from Brief 30 baseline (per `pnpm --filter @splash/web build`: `/admin/sysadmin` 7.06 kB / 112 kB; `/admin/damage/[id]` 3.08 kB / 108 kB; `/admin/pricing/[location]` 3.65 kB / 109 kB; `/admin/dashboard` + `/admin/damage` + `/admin/pricing` 167 B / 105 kB each — all matching the post-Brief-30 snapshot). Next.js code-splits `error.tsx` boundary components into a separate chunk loaded only when the boundary fires, so the main route bundle stays the same size. The boundary chunk itself is well under 1 kB (one client component, two render branches, minimal JSX). (f) **Next 15.5.15 honors `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` natively** — the brief asked to confirm; verified against Next.js 14.2+ behaviour (the env var has been a documented stability lever since 14.2). No experimental-block plumbing required in `next.config.mjs`. (g) **Out-of-scope items in the brief stay out-of-scope:** Cloudflare Insights beacon SRI failure (analytics dropout, not a functional issue), the unrelated 404 on `/admin/damage/VES-20260505-181329-PLUF` (stale prefetch from earlier testing), and the broader migration away from server actions toward client-side `fetch()`. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.625s (5 cached + 8 fresh; the workers were not edited, so their fresh runs are turbo-baseline behaviour for this session). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 7.3s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** zero observable change vs. post-Brief-30 snapshot (error boundary code-split into its own chunk; loaded on-demand only). | Claude Code (Brief 31 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 30 completed:** Sysadmin UX overhaul — flat 8-card list reorganized into a two-mode hub (Manage Users / Manage Tables) with operations split into per-mode section files; new `GET /sysadmin/api/audit-log` endpoint and inline filterable Activity log panel surface `sysadmin_audit_log` content for the first time. **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — header comment lists the new endpoint; `OWNED_GET_PATHS` extended with `/sysadmin/api/audit-log`; top-level fetch dispatch routes the path to a new `handleSearchAuditLog`. The handler validates filter params (allow-list on `action` and `target_type`; UUID regex on `user_id`; lowercase regex on `location_code`; `Date.parse` shape check on `since`/`until`; ilike-sanitization drops `,()*%_` from the actor substring), composes the PostgREST query with implicit AND across constraints, paginates via `limit` (default 50, max 200) + `offset`, orders `created_at.desc`, sends `Prefer: count=estimated` and parses the trailing number off the `Content-Range` header for `total_estimate`, and returns `{ rows, total_estimate, next_offset }` (next_offset null when fewer rows returned than limit). The `user_id` filter pins target_type to `user_permissions`/`user_tool_access`/`auth.users`; the `location_code` filter pins target_type to `pricing_simple`/`locations` and matches `target_id` eq or `code/%` (the second arm catches Brief 26's composite IDs like `binghamton/bath`). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — full rewrite. Reads `searchParams.mode` (default "users"); renders `<PageBanner>` (subtitle replaced with "Manage users and tables, with full activity history below." — the previous meta-commentary about `sysadmin_audit_log` was dropped per the brief), `<ModePicker>`, the active section (`<UserOperations>` or `<TableOperations>`), and `<AuditLogPanel>` always-visible at the bottom. The 5-card Brief-7 cards + Brief 24/26/27's table-mgmt cards are no longer hosted in `page.tsx`. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 30 row marked Completed (2026-05-05). `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` — Brief 30 line moved to the completed-tombstone block. `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry extended to describe the two-mode hub, the audit-log surface, and the URL search-param conventions (`?mode=users\|tables`, `?audit_actor=`, `?audit_action=`, `?audit_table=`, `?audit_user_id=`, `?audit_location_code=`, `?audit_offset=`). `BUILD_STATE.md` — Last updated bumped, this entry, sysadmin row in the apps/web pages table updated to reflect the new structure + bundle size, prioritized work list row 30 added with full one-line summary. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/OperationCard.tsx` — extracts the shared `<details>`-based card primitive + `FieldLabel` + `inputClass`/`submitClass` tokens from the old `page.tsx` so both section files can re-import without duplication. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/ModePicker.tsx` — server-component picker (Option A). Two `<Link>` buttons (no JS, no client state); the active mode gets filled splash-blue background + `aria-current="page"`. Hrefs preserve every existing search param except `mode` itself, so the operator's audit-log filters survive a mode swap. Static "5 operations" / "3 operations" subtitles per the brief. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx` — server component rendering 5 user-mgmt cards (Create user / Set role / Grant tool / Revoke tool / Reset password). Lifted verbatim from the old `page.tsx`; the document-delegated `PASSWORD_MATCH_SCRIPT` came along with the Reset-password card. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/TableOperations.tsx` — server component rendering 3 table-mgmt cards wrapping the existing client islands (`AddLocationCard` / `UpdatePackageCard` / `UpdateLocationCard`). Card titles + descriptions verbatim from the old `page.tsx`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` — server component. Reads `audit_actor` / `audit_action` / `audit_table` / `audit_user_id` / `audit_location_code` / `audit_offset` from `searchParams`; calls `sysadminGetJson<AuditLogResponse>` (the existing service-binding-with-URL-fallback helper) against the new endpoint. Renders a filter row above the table (GET-method `<form>` posting back to `/admin/sysadmin`; preserves the active mode via a hidden input; resets `audit_offset` to 0 on every submit) with: actor text input, action select (allow-list + "(any)"), table select (allow-list + "(any)"), `<UserPicker name="audit_user_id">` (reuses Brief 18's client island), location_code text input with `pattern="[a-z0-9_]*"`. Below the form: a compact 5-column table (When / Actor / Action / Target / Diff) with offset pagination ("Showing N–M of ~XYZ" + "Load more" link bumping `?audit_offset=`). Empty state: italic "No audit entries match these filters." Error state (worker call throws or returns null on 401/403): scoped `role="alert"` banner; the rest of the page keeps rendering. Per-row details: When = relative time (server-rendered, ISO timestamp on title attr); Actor italicizes when `actor_email === "system"`; Action mono + `_noop` actions get a `(no-op)` pill; Target = `target_type/target_id` with UUIDs truncated to first 8 chars + ellipsis + full id on title attr; Diff = `<details>` summary "View" → before/after JSON in `<pre>` blocks side-by-side (or stacked on mobile via `md:grid-cols-2`), inserts show only "after", deletes show only "before", `notes` rendered as italic small text below. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Option A (URL search-param-driven mode picker) chosen** per the brief's recommendation — clean URL, deep-linkable, no extra JS, plays nicely with the existing server-rendered + `router.refresh()` pattern. (2) **Relative-time formatter is server-rendered**, not a client island — sufficient for v1 because the page only updates on refresh; client hydration would add weight (a `RelativeTime.tsx` `"use client"` component is a follow-up if the operator wants tick-by-tick updates). The absolute ISO timestamp lives on the title attr for hover. (3) **`AuditLogPanel` always renders for both modes** — the brief's two-mode hub diagram shows the panel at page bottom regardless of mode; operators commonly want to verify a write they just made via the mutation UI without flipping modes. (4) **Filter changes always reset `audit_offset` to 0** via a hidden `<input type="hidden" name="audit_offset" value="0">` in the filter form — pagination only makes sense relative to the current filter set; preserving it across filter changes leads to confusing empty pages. (5) **Reset link clears every `audit_*` param but keeps `mode`** — operators expect to reset the log without losing their current mode context. (6) **`UserPicker` reused as-is for the user filter** — passes `name="audit_user_id"` instead of `name="user_id"`; on initial render with `audit_user_id` already in the URL, the picker is given `defaultValue` + a placeholder `defaultLabel="(filtering — clear to change)"` because we can't roundtrip the email server-side without a second worker call (and the picker's existing contract uses `defaultLabel` for display). Operators clear + retype to change. (7) **`AuditLogResponse.next_offset` is computed server-side as `rows.length < limit ? null : offset + rows.length`** — simpler for the panel to render "Load more" conditional vs. computing it locally. (8) **No CSV export, no live updates, no JSON structural diff** per brief out-of-scope. (9) **No audit-log entry written for the audit-log read** — explicitly out-of-scope; reading the log is a super_admin observation. (10) **Allowed-action list hardcoded in both worker and apps/web filter UI**; future briefs that add a new audit `action` string need to update both lists. The worker rejects unknown action filter values with 400, which is a useful early-warning if a typo or a forgotten audit constant slips through. (11) **`OperationCard` extracted to `_components/`, not `_sections/`**, because both sections re-import it; section files are mode-specific containers, components are reusable primitives. (12) **No bottom-of-section "Refresh" button** — the operator can hard-refresh, and `router.refresh()` is already triggered by every successful mutation via `<ActionForm>`. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`PostgREST` allows duplicate top-level filter params, AND-combining them**, which is what we lean on when `user_id` is set (it adds `target_type=in.(...)` AND `target_id=eq.{uuid}` ON TOP of any explicit `table=...` filter). If the operator selects table=`pricing_simple` + a user_id, the result is empty (correct, since user_id rows can't have target_type=`pricing_simple`) — UX could surface that more obviously, e.g. as an inline "filter combo yields nothing" hint. v1 just shows the empty state. (b) **`audit_user_id` round-trip**: when filtered, the picker shows a stale "(filtering — clear to change)" instead of the email. Resolving it would need a second worker call (`/sysadmin/api/users?id=<uuid>`) which the worker doesn't currently support. Follow-up brief if the operator finds this confusing. (c) **`Content-Range` header** is read off the response and parsed for the trailing total. If Supabase ever stops sending the header (Prefer ignored, schema misconfig), `total_estimate` falls through to null and the panel renders "Showing 1–N" without the total. The Load-more link still works (next_offset is computed from rows.length, independent of the header). (d) **Schema verification**: I trusted the brief's inferred shape for `sysadmin_audit_log` (id, created_at, actor_id, actor_email, action, target_type, target_id, before, after, notes). I didn't query the DB to verify — the audit handler reads `select=*` so any extra columns flow through harmlessly to the rendered diff. The rendered table only references the documented columns. (e) **`location_code` filter regex is `[a-z0-9_]+`** matching the worker's `LOCATION_CODE_RE`. The HTML input's `pattern="[a-z0-9_]*"` is a UX hint (also accepts empty so submit-without-filter doesn't fail). (f) **No worker-side rate limiting on audit-log GETs** — the endpoint is super_admin-gated, so the operator headcount caps the abuse surface. (g) **Bundle delta within estimate.** `/admin/sysadmin` post-Brief-30: **7.06 kB / 112 kB First Load JS** (was **6.94 kB / 112 kB** post-Brief-27) — **+0.12 kB / 0 kB**. The audit panel is server-rendered; the only client weight added is one extra `UserPicker` mount in the filter row + a couple of additional `<Link>` instances. Comfortably under the brief's "audit panel + JSON diff rendering will likely add weight" caveat. (h) **No new audit-log action strings observed** beyond the allow-list during static review (couldn't query live data from headless mode); operators should flag any unknown action that surfaces a 400 from the filter UI as either a typo or a forgotten future-feature constant. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 4.956s (11 cached + 2 fresh — `@splash/sysadmin-worker` and `@splash/web` invalidated). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.6s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/sysadmin` 6.94 kB → **7.06 kB** / 112 kB → 112 kB First Load JS (+0.12 kB / 0 kB). All other route bundles unchanged from Brief 29 snapshot. | Claude Code (Brief 30 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 29 completed:** three post-test fixes from operator end-to-end testing earlier today. **(A) Tab title cosmetic.** `apps/web/app/layout.tsx` line 28 flipped from `title: "Splash MaxPass"` to `title: "Splash Tools"`. Audit of worker `<title>` tags surfaced only customer-facing strings: `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` (Vehicle Issue Report, Claim Submitted, Location Not Found — all left as-is per brief scope, customer-facing and location-specific); `apps/signup-worker/src/render/picker.ts` and `apps/signup-worker/src/render/form.ts` (location-specific Choose Package / Sign Up titles — left as-is, customer signup flow). No other admin-tool tab titles found anywhere in `apps/`. The `metadata.title` field on the Next.js root layout was the single admin-tool tab title in the entire monorepo. **(B) Add Location now writes locations row + accepts address.** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` `handleCreateLocation`: new `address` body field (optional, trimmed, empty→null); `site` is now required and must parse to a positive integer (used as `locations.site_number`); new pre-check on `locations?site_number=eq.{n}` returns 409 "Location already exists in locations table" on hit; new `LocationsInsertRow` payload (site_number, site=location_pretty, location=address, area_manager, regional_manager, am_email, rm_email, site_email) POSTed to `/rest/v1/locations` with `Prefer: return=representation` so the inserted `id` round-trips back; pricing_simple INSERT now happens AFTER and includes `address` as a new field on each row (the `PricingSimpleInsertRow` interface gained an `address: string \| null` member); on pricing_simple insert failure the worker issues a best-effort `DELETE /rest/v1/locations?id=eq.{insertedId}` rollback (the locations row is the only thing committed at that point); if the rollback DELETE also fails it logs an audit row with `action: "create_location_failed"`, `needs_manual_cleanup: true`, and `orphan_location_id` so the operator knows manual cleanup is required, then surfaces both errors in the 500 response. The successful-path audit row's `after` payload now includes `location_id`, `site_number`, and `address` alongside the existing `location_code` / `location_pretty` / `package_count`. Worker response shape gained `location_id` and `site_number` fields. apps/web `AddLocationCard`: `site` input gained `required` + `inputMode="numeric"` + `pattern="[0-9]+"`; helper text reworded to "Positive integer. Used as locations.site_number." A new `address` input was added to the location-level grid with helper text "Street address. Stored on locations row + propagated to pricing_simple.address." apps/web `createLocationAction`: forwards the new `address` field as `body.address`; success message now reads `Location created: <code> (#<site_number>, <package_count> packages)`. **(C) Claim form overlay clear on success.** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` `FORM_SCRIPT.showOutcome`: `setSubmitting(false)` added as the FIRST line, before the `formPage.hidden = true; outcomePage.hidden = false;` lines. This hides the previously-orphaned `#submittingOverlay` div that sits OUTSIDE both #formPage and #outcomePage and was never being toggled off on the success path. Verified `showOutcome` is called from exactly one site (`form.addEventListener('submit', ...).then(...).if (out.ok && out.body && out.body.ok)` branch) — no other call sites; the early `setSubmitting(false)` is idempotent regardless. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/layout.tsx` (1 line), `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` (1 line in showOutcome), `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` (handleCreateLocation extended with locations pre-check + INSERT + rollback DELETE on pricing_simple failure; new `LocationsInsertRow` interface; `PricingSimpleInsertRow` gained `address` field; header comment updated to reflect Brief 29's locations-row addition), `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AddLocationCard.tsx` (site → required + numeric pattern + reworded help text; new address input with helper text), `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` (`CreateLocationBody` interface gained `address?: string \| null`; `createLocationAction` reads address + forwards in body; success message includes `#site_number` and reads `respSiteNumber` from worker response), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (row 29 marked Completed; link points at `brief-029-...md`, the canonical filename after cowork's renaming), `CLAUDE.md` (sysadmin glossary entry extended to describe Brief 29's locations-row + address behaviour and the rollback-on-failure flow). **Files created:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`address` lands in both `locations.location` and `pricing_simple.address` on initial INSERT** rather than relying on the trigger to backfill — the trigger fires `ON locations AFTER UPDATE` (not INSERT) per Brief 27's spec, so a fresh INSERT wouldn't propagate without a follow-up UPDATE. (2) **`locations.site` gets `location_pretty`, not the form's `site` text input** — per the brief's payload spec; the type confirms `locations.site` is the display name (e.g. "Binghamton") while `site_number` is the integer. `pricing_simple.site` is left unchanged (still receives `stringOrNull(body.site)`, the form's site number text). (3) **`location_code`-on-locations pre-check skipped** — the type (`packages/types/src/locations.ts`) confirms `locations` doesn't carry a `location_code` column; only `site_number` is the unique business key on that side. The brief's "whichever column locations actually uses for code" caveat resolved to "site_number only." (4) **Best-effort rollback DELETE captures the inserted id from the `Prefer: return=representation` response** — the worker reads `locInsertedRows[0]!.id` straight off the POST response, so even if Supabase doesn't return an id (older/configured-off case) the worker degrades gracefully (`needs_manual_cleanup: true`, `orphan_location_id: null` in audit). (5) **`needs_manual_cleanup` audit row is best-effort with try/catch wrapping** — if the audit insert ALSO fails (cascading failure), we don't want to mask the original 500 with a third error; the audit failure is silently swallowed and the 500 still surfaces both the pricing_simple error and the cleanup DELETE error. (6) **Site-number `pattern="[0-9]+"` on the form input is a UX hint only** — server-side validation in `handleCreateLocation` does the real `Number.isInteger() && > 0` check; the `pattern` just nudges the browser keypad on mobile. (7) **`address` is optional in the worker** but the form input doesn't carry `required` — operators may leave address blank for new locations whose street address isn't known yet. (8) **Brief filename / numbering quirk surfaced**: the brief was originally filed under `BRIEFS/brief-028-tab-title-add-location-row-claim-overlay.md` and self-titled "Brief 28", but BUILD_STATE.md row 28 was already reserved for the still-open cross-worker cache invalidation brief. The cowork tooling renumbered the live brief to Brief 29 (file is `brief-029-...md`; `brief-028-...md` is now a one-line tombstone pointing at 029). Resolution: the new BUILD_STATE.md prioritized work list row is numbered 29 (matching INDEX.md), INDEX.md row 29's link points at `brief-029-...md`, and BUILD_STATE.md row 28 (cache invalidation) is unchanged. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No locations.site_number unique constraint verified at the DB level.** The pre-check returns 409 if a row already exists, but if the constraint isn't enforced (or is enforced under a different name), a TOCTOU race between two simultaneous create-location calls could insert two rows with the same site_number. Negligible at single-operator headcount; flag if multi-operator concurrent create becomes a thing. (b) **`trg_sync_pricing_simple` trigger fires `ON locations AFTER UPDATE`, not `AFTER INSERT`.** Brief 29's INSERT path doesn't trigger the cascade, but that's by design — pricing_simple is populated explicitly by the same handler with the denormalized fields. Future locations-side UPDATEs (via Brief 27) will fire the trigger and overwrite pricing_simple's denormalized columns to match locations. (c) **`pricing_simple.site` still receives the form's site text (e.g. "34"), but `locations.site` receives `location_pretty` ("Binghamton")** — the two columns named `site` mean different things by schema design. If `trg_sync_pricing_simple` syncs locations.site INTO pricing_simple.site on UPDATE, a Brief 27 site-edit would replace pricing_simple.site's number-string with the display name. This is consistent with the existing trigger spec (Brief 26 documents pricing_simple.site as locations-synced) but worth re-verifying once the operator exercises the full create-then-update cycle in production. (d) **`address` values are not normalized.** Whatever the operator types is what gets stored in both columns. No address-shape validation, no zip parsing, no postal-service lookup. (location, area_manager, regional_manager — no permissions-side cascade), and "Locations table only — no cascade." (hrt_email, rm_group). (2) **`mla_location` is shown read-only in the context block** rather than added as a 10th editable field. The brief's editable list doesn't include it; a future brief can promote it if operators ask. (3) **`hrt_email` is rendered alongside the cascading email fields** with a distinct "Locations table only — no cascade" hint so the operator can edit it without thinking it propagates anywhere. Same for `rm_group`. (4) **Empty form value → null** for every editable field. Clearing the column (rather than leaving the prior value untouched when blank) is the more predictable behaviour because the operator's mental model is "what I see is what gets saved." The worker accepts null on every editable field. (5) **Three help-text variants instead of one repeated hint** — visually distinguishes which fields cascade where. The worker passes `select=*` through verbatim, so the type stays accurate even if the schema evolves; apps/web only consumes the columns the picker + read-only block reference. The worker accepts that; intended behaviour. (b) **`created_at` / `updated_at` columns on `locations` are assumed to exist.** The brief's Read-only-fields section lists them; I show them in the read-only context block. The Supabase locations type in `packages/types/src/locations.ts` doesn't declare them (the type was inherited from the legacy schema, which omits audit columns). If they don't actually exist on the table, the read-only block renders em-dashes — no error. (c) **`hrt_email` and `rm_group` are confirmed real** per `packages/types/src/locations.ts:27,24`; same for the 4 manager/email fields the brief lists. The brief's "if columns don't exist, hide them" caveat is moot — they all exist. If the trigger names differ or are missing, the cascade won't fire — the worker's PATCH still succeeds (it's just an UPDATE on locations); operator would notice via stale pricing_simple. Smoke-test the cascade by editing an email and checking pricing_simple in production. (f) **TOCTOU on selector** — between the existence pre-check and the PATCH, another super_admin could delete the row. Worst case: PATCH affects 0 rows; Supabase returns `[]` and we surface a 200 with `after = null`. No data corruption. Negligible at operator headcount. | 2026-05-05 | **Brief 26 completed:** Sysadmin Update Package — search-then-edit one `pricing_simple` row by composite PK (`location_code`, `pkg`), second of three pricing_simple/locations management features. **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — header comment lists two new endpoints (GET `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search` + PATCH `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/package`); new `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS` set; `OWNED_GET_PATHS` extended; top-level `fetch()` dispatch refactored to (a) reject the request when the path is not in any owned set, (b) enforce method per category (POST → POST, PATCH → PATCH, GET → GET), (c) extend the `isOriginAllowed` CSRF gate to PATCH (state-changing) in addition to POST, (d) route GET path `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search` to the new `handleSearchPricingSimple`, (e) route PATCH path to the new `handleUpdatePackage`. New `handleSearchPricingSimple` — empty/whitespace q → `[]`; sanitises q against PostgREST `or()` separators (`,()*%_`); raw fetch to Supabase REST `pricing_simple?or=(location_code.ilike.%q%,location_pretty.ilike.%q%,site.ilike.%q%)&order=location_code.asc,sort.asc,pkg.asc&limit=50` with service-role headers; returns the array verbatim. New `handleUpdatePackage` — validates composite PK (location_code regex `[a-z0-9_]+`, pkg non-empty); rejects any of the six denormalized fields (`area_manager`, `regional_manager`, `am_email`, `rm_email`, `site_email`, `address`) with 400 + a friendly "edit via Update Location" message; coerces editable fields into a partial-PATCH payload (`pkg$` required, `single`/`flash2`/`flash5`/`sort` accept null when blank, `pricing` constrained to the five `PRICING_MODES`, `location_pretty` non-empty when present); optional `pkg_new` rename validated for non-emptiness and only applied when it differs from `pkg`; refuses an empty payload with 400 ("No editable fields supplied"). Pre-checks: `select=*` SELECT 1 by composite PK returning 404 "Package not found" on miss, captures the row for the `before` audit snapshot; if renaming, second SELECT against `(location_code, pkg_new)` returning 409 "Target package name already exists" on collision. Issues a single PATCH to `${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/pricing_simple?location_code=eq.X&pkg=eq.Y` with `Prefer: return=representation` so the response carries the after-row; audit log entry `action: "update_package"`, `target_id: "${location_code}/${final_pkg}"`, `before` = pre-PATCH row, `after` = post-PATCH row. Returns `{ ok, location_code, pkg: <new-or-unchanged>, updated_at }`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` — header comment extended with Brief 26 paragraph; `sysadminPostJson` refactored to delegate to a new private `sysadminWriteJson(method, ...)`; new public `sysadminPatchJson` thin-wraps the same helper with method=PATCH (avoids 70-line copy-paste); error-message string switched from hardcoded "POST" to the active method. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — header comment lists Brief 26 + the new endpoint; import adds `sysadminPatchJson`; new `updatePackageAction` reads `location_code`/`pkg_original`/`pkg`/`location_pretty`/`pricing`/`pkg_dollar` plus the four nullable numeric fields, builds an `UpdatePackageBody` with literal `"pkg$"` key, sets `pkg_new` only when the editable `pkg` differs from `pkg_original`, coerces blanks to `null` for nullable columns, validates `sort` as a positive integer when present, calls `sysadminPatchJson("/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/package", body)`, surfaces `{ ok: true, message: "Updated <code>/<pkg>" }` (using the worker's response body for the post-rename pkg) or `{ ok: false, error }`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — header lists 7 cards + Brief 26; `UpdatePackageCard` import added; new `UpdatePackageOperationCard` server component wraps the client island in the existing `OperationCard` shell. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 26 row marked Completed (2026-05-05). `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry rewritten: keeps the Brief 24 Add Location summary, adds the Brief 26 Update Package summary including the editable-vs-rejected field split driven by the `trg_sync_pricing_simple` trigger, points at Brief 27 (not yet started) for the denormalized-field path, repeats the cross-worker cache invalidation TODO. `BUILD_STATE.md` — Last updated bumped, this entry, sysadmin row in the apps/web pages table updated to reflect 7 cards + new bundle size, prioritized work list row 26 marked **completed** with full one-line summary. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/PackageSearchPicker.tsx` — client island. Mirrors `UserPicker` (Brief 18) shape: 250ms debounced fetch to `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search?q=...`, dropdown with combobox/listbox aria roles + arrow key nav + Esc + outside-click dismiss, error banner on non-2xx. Differences from UserPicker: (1) selection state is held by the **parent** (UpdatePackageCard) via `selected` + `onSelect` props rather than internally, because the parent needs the full row data to inflate the form; (2) dropdown rows render `${location_code}/${pkg}` mono-bold on the left + an "Updated YYYY-MM-DD" badge on the right + a secondary line with the location_pretty + price summary `$pkg$/single $single`; (3) selection chip shows the composite PK + price summary instead of an email. Exports `PricingSimpleSearchRow` interface for the parent's typing. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/UpdatePackageCard.tsx` — client island. Owns selection state via `useState<PricingSimpleSearchRow \| null>`. Renders the picker + a "pick a package above" hint when nothing's selected; on selection inflates the editable form (8 fields in a 2-col grid: `pkg`, `location_pretty`, `pkg$`, `single`, `flash2`, `flash5`, `sort`, `pricing`) plus a `ReadOnlyContext` block listing the 9 denormalized/audit fields with an italic note "Edits here would be reverted by the sync trigger." Form has hidden inputs for `location_code` (composite PK) + `pkg_original` (the pkg name at selection time so the worker knows what to look up before applying any rename). Wraps in `<ActionForm action={updatePackageAction} resetOnSuccess={false} onResult={...}>` — `resetOnSuccess={false}` because the form is keyed on the selection and we clear selection in `onResult` on success (the form unmounts when selection becomes null, simpler than relying on key-trick remount with stale defaultValues). Cancel link clears the selection without submitting. Form uses `key={location_code/pkg}` so picking a different row remounts and re-binds defaultValues. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker uses PATCH semantically + a new `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS` dispatch set** rather than reusing POST. The brief explicitly listed "PATCH" first and offered POST only as a fallback "if sysadminPostJson only does POST". The dispatch refactor is small (5 added lines + 1 method rename) and matches REST convention; the downstream Supabase REST call is also PATCH so the verbs line up end-to-end. The CSRF `isOriginAllowed` gate now applies to both POST and PATCH for symmetry. (2) **`sysadminWriteJson(method, path, body)` extracted from sysadminPostJson** rather than duplicating the 70-line dual-mode body for sysadminPatchJson. Behaviour is identical except for the HTTP method passed through the service-binding fetch and the URL fallback fetch, plus the error-message string. The public sysadminPostJson signature is preserved. (3) **PackageSearchPicker takes selection via props from the parent**, not held internally — this keeps the parent in control of "is the form inflated" state and avoids the picker needing to drive a sibling form's mount. UserPicker holds its own selection because the surrounding form just needs a hidden user_id; PackageSearchPicker's surrounding form needs the entire row to pre-fill 8+ inputs, which is impractical to thread through hidden inputs. (4) **Read-only context block uses `<dl>` semantics** with a 2-column grid; mono on `location_code` + `updated_at`, plain on the rest. Empty/null values render as a faded em-dash so missing data is visually distinct from blank-on-purpose. (5) **`pricing` mode select is required and pre-defaults to "full"** when the row's pricing column is null — the pricing column is non-nullable in the schema (Brief 24's create-location hardcodes "full"), so this is defensive only. Operator can switch between any of the 5 valid modes (`full`, `same`, `flash5`, `flash2`, `special`). (6) **`single`, `flash2`, `flash5`, `sort` accept blank → null** because the schema permits null on these (the brief's editable-fields spec listed them as nullable). `pkg$` and `pkg` are required (`pkg$` is the headline price, `pkg` is half the composite PK). `location_pretty` is technically schema-non-null but the action only sends it when present; an empty form-input value is filtered out and the worker leaves the column unchanged. (7) **`pkg_new` is sent only when the editable `pkg` differs from `pkg_original`** — sending `pkg_new === pkg` would force the worker into the rename branch unnecessarily (one extra collision pre-check). The action elides it when equal. (8) **On save success, the parent clears selection** (via `onResult` callback) rather than re-fetching the row to refresh values. The picker returns to its empty state; operator picks the next package. `revalidatePath("/admin/sysadmin")` + `router.refresh()` re-render the page server side, but client-side selection state is cleared explicitly. This avoids "save shows old defaultValue" UX confusion. (9) **`OperationCard` description copy** mentions the editable fields directly (`pkg$, single, flash2/5, sort, pkg name, pricing mode`) so a super_admin sees what the card edits without expanding it. (10) **Cancel button is `type="button"` with onClick**, not a `<a href>` — keeps the operator on the same scroll position and avoids a full page navigation. (11) **`PRICING_MODE_OPTIONS` hardcoded as a const tuple** rather than imported from `@splash/types`'s `PRICING_MODES` — `@splash/types` is transpiled by Next but importing a value (vs. type-only) into a client component pulls the whole module's exports into the bundle; the 5-string constant is cheaper inline. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-worker cache invalidation gap (TODO)** — same as Brief 24. signup-worker caches `pricing_simple_resolved` for 5 minutes; package edits surface on the customer signup form within that window without operator action. A code-comment TODO points at the gap in `handleUpdatePackage`. (b) **`pkg_original` is operator-supplied via the form**, not a server-stored token — a malicious super_admin could edit a different row by lying in the hidden input, but a malicious super_admin can already issue any SQL via Supabase. The composite PK is the only auth-relevant identifier and it's checked at the worker. Acceptable for v1. (c) **Read-only context fields show what the picker fetched at selection time** — they're not re-read during edit. If the operator edits a package and another super_admin updates the location's am_email in a parallel session, the read-only block is stale until the next picker pick. Acceptable v1; a future enhancement could re-fetch on every Save. (d) **Rename pre-check has a TOCTOU window** — a concurrent rename to the same target name could slip in between the collision SELECT and the PATCH. Worst case: Supabase returns a constraint violation 500 wrapped in our `Supabase update failed: ...` shape. No data corruption (the constraint catches it). Negligible in practice given the operator headcount. (e) **`updated_at` audit column is auto-managed by the schema's row-update trigger** (the existing pattern for pricing_simple) — the worker doesn't set it explicitly. The PATCH response with `Prefer: return=representation` reflects whatever the trigger produced, which is what we surface in the action's success message and audit log. (f) **The brief's spec for the search return shape included `address` as a top-level field on `PricingSimpleSearchRow`**, which the worker's GET endpoint passes through verbatim from Supabase. If `pricing_simple` doesn't actually have an `address` column (the schema is the source of truth and I didn't verify), the field comes back as `undefined` and the read-only context block renders an em-dash. No type error because the rows are typed `unknown`-passthrough at the worker boundary; the apps/web TS interface declares it `string | null` but null + undefined render identically in `ReadOnlyField`. Verify in production smoke. (g) **`pkg_dollar` form-field name is mapped to `"pkg$"` JSON key in the action** — same convention Brief 24 used (HTML form-name avoids the `$` to sidestep tooling that might choke on it; the action does the rename once). (h) **Bundle delta within estimate.** `/admin/sysadmin` post-Brief-26: **5.6 kB / 111 kB First Load JS** (was **3.93 kB / 109 kB** post-Brief-24) — **+1.67 kB / +2 kB**, in line with the brief's "+1-2 kB" estimate. PackageSearchPicker (~1 kB) + UpdatePackageCard inflate state (~0.6 kB) account for the delta; the form pre-fill machinery is mostly defaultValue strings which compress well. Adds (1) `vehicleYear` `required` attribute; (2) "Managers only" amber pill rendered next to the `customer_get_quotes` determination radio via a new `managersOnly: boolean` flag on the `DETERMINATION_CHOICES` rows + new `.pill` / `.pill-warn` CSS; (3) equipment yes/no segmented toggle defaulting to No that conditionally reveals the equipment dropdown + the malfunction checkbox (when No, `equipmentInvolved` is forced to empty string so the worker's existing `equipmentRelated = (equipmentInvolved && !== "N/A") ? 1 : 0` derivation maps to 0); (4) PIN-gated employee section with a custom overlay modal (PIN=1981 hardcoded as customer-visible obfuscation per brief; correct PIN hides the "Continue" button + reveals the employee section + submit row); (5) multi-photo widget for fourCornersPhotos + damagePhotos with thumbnail previews via `URL.createObjectURL`, per-thumb Remove links, and "+ Add another photo" button labels; single-photo widget for vinPhoto + platePhoto with "Replace photo" label after the first add; (6) `capture` attribute dropped from every photo input — browser-native chooser between camera and gallery; (7) JS-driven submit with `e.preventDefault()`, `FormData` construction (text fields via `new FormData(form)`, photos appended from JS-tracked `photos` object under canonical worker field names), `fetch` with `Accept: application/json`, "Submitting claim, please wait..." overlay, success outcome card showing `claim_id` in monospace + "Submit another claim" reload button, inline error banner above the form on non-2xx. The `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES` tuple lost the explicit "N/A" entry — the toggle's No state is now the canonical "no equipment involved" path. Inline JS lives in a module-level `FORM_SCRIPT` constant (string concatenation throughout — no JS template literals — so the outer TS template literal cannot interpolate `${...}` patterns by accident); interpolated once into the rendered HTML via `<script>${FORM_SCRIPT}</script>`. `<form novalidate>` defers all validation to the JS submit handler so HTML5 bubbles don't fire on the "Continue" button click path. `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — `handleClaimSubmission` JSON success branch now returns `{ ok: true, claim_id, ...legacy success/claimId/powerAutomateSuccess/d1Success/photosUploaded keys }`; failure branch returns `{ ok: false, error, success: false }` with status 500. Browser-mode redirect path (`Accept: text/html`) unchanged from Brief 23. **Files created:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`equipmentMalfunction` checkbox folded inside the equipment-yes block** rather than left as an always-visible top-level toggle — the malfunction question only makes sense when equipment was actually involved; toggle-No forces the hidden `equipmentMalfunction` input to "false". (2) **`employeeName` is required only after PIN unlock**, set via JS on the unlock event — keeping `required` on a hidden field would fire the bubble on the Continue path or before the operator could see the field. (3) **PIN gate uses a custom overlay div, not `<dialog>`** — broader browser compat (older iOS Safari especially), simpler styling, and no polyfill needed. Cancel + Continue buttons + Enter-key submit; wrong PIN shows inline error and clears the input; no rate limiting (PIN is obfuscation, not auth). (4) **JSON success shape mirrors both `ok`/`claim_id` AND legacy `success`/`claimId`** rather than picking one — the form's JS reads `body.claim_id || body.claimId`; future cleanup can drop the legacy keys when the operator confirms no programmatic caller is parsing them. (5) **Photo widget uses one hidden `<input type="file">` per section** rather than a single shared input that gets re-targeted — simpler event wiring, no race between concurrent clicks. `accept="image/*"` only; no `capture`; no `multiple` (one-at-a-time UX is deliberate per brief). (6) **`URL.createObjectURL` thumbnails are revoked on re-render** — hygiene, not load-bearing for short-lived form sessions. (7) **Submit-time validation order:** sync the equipment-toggle's conditional `required` first, then `form.checkValidity()` (browser bubble for missing required fields), then JS-side photo-count validation since photos aren't native form inputs anymore. Photo-missing errors render in the inline error banner above the form. (8) **"Submit another claim" reset is `window.location.reload()`** per brief — simplest correct path; the JS state machine (photos object, thumbUrls, PIN-gate visibility, equipment toggle) doesn't have a single reset hook. (9) **`form.action="/claims-api/submit-claim"` retained** even though submit is JS-driven — graceful degradation if JS is disabled (extremely unlikely in 2026 but cheap to keep). (10) **`novalidate` on `<form>`** — defers validation to the explicit `validateBeforeSubmit()` so HTML5 bubbles don't fire on the Continue button click path or while the employee section is hidden. (11) **Equipment "N/A" choice removed from the dropdown** — the toggle's No state is the canonical "no equipment involved" path; keeping "N/A" alongside Yes would be redundant and confusing. The worker's `!== "N/A"` clause stays intact (defense in depth) but no longer has anything to match. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No client-side photo size cap** — the worker delegates to the CF Workers 100 MB request body limit; a customer with a stack of HEIF originals could theoretically exceed that. Pre-resize via canvas or HEIC→JPEG conversion is out of scope per brief. (b) **PIN=1981 is in the worker bundle and rendered HTML's `<script>`** — visible in DevTools. Brief explicitly classified this as obfuscation, not auth. If a real auth flow lands later, a separate brief replaces this with a Supabase short-lived token gate. (c) **Submit overlay is a full-viewport opaque-ish overlay**, not a spinner — kept simple. If submission stalls (slow upload), the operator sees only "Submitting claim, please wait...". Future polish: progress indicator showing upload percentage. (d) **`form.checkValidity()` includes still-hidden fields** — mitigated by the linear PIN gate flow (employee section is the only conditionally-hidden block, and the submit button is hidden until PIN unlock); the conditional-required pattern on `equipmentInvolved` (toggled true only when equipment-yes is selected) handles the in-section conditional case. (e) **Worker JSON failure does NOT include partial state** (whether D1 succeeded, whether PA POSTed) — only `body.error`. If a future smoke test wants per-stage diagnostics for the customer-facing form, add an `?debug=1` opt-in branch. (f) **CSRF posture unchanged** — `POST /claims-api/submit-claim` remains public, no `isOriginAllowed` (intentional per Brief 23). The JSON failure shape change doesn't widen the surface. (g) **Bundle delta within estimate.** Damage-worker post-Brief-25: **1644.95 KiB / 372.70 KiB gzip** (was **1628.16 KiB / 368.53 KiB gzip** post-Brief-23) — **+16.79 KiB raw / +4.17 KiB gzip**, within the brief's 5-10 KiB estimate; comfortably under the 3 MiB compressed CF Workers free-tier limit. Driven by the inline `FORM_SCRIPT` (PIN gate + photo widget + equipment toggle + JS submit + outcome card) plus added CSS for the new visual components. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 1.906s (12 cached + 1 fresh — only `@splash/damage-worker` invalidated by the renderClaimForm rewrite + index.ts JSON shape edit; all other packages cache-hit). `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded. Bindings inventoried: DB / R2_BUCKET / IMAGES. | Claude Code (Brief 25 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 24 completed:** Sysadmin Add Location feature — direct pricing_simple bulk insert from `/admin/sysadmin`, first of three planned pricing_simple/locations management features (Briefs 25 + 26 deferred). **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — header comment block updated to list the new endpoint; `OWNED_POST_PATHS` extended with `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/create-location`; new switch arm dispatches to `handleCreateLocation`; new `handleCreateLocation` function with full validation per spec (location_pretty non-empty, location_code regex `/^[a-z0-9_]+$/`, uniqueness pre-check via Supabase REST `pricing_simple?location_code=eq.<code>&limit=1` returning 409 "Location code already in use" on hit, packages array length >= 1, per-package validation of pkg non-empty + pkg$/single/flash2/flash5 non-negative numbers + sort positive integer when present + duplicate-pkg detection, optional email shape validation `/^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$/` for site_email/am_email/rm_email); on valid input, builds a `PricingSimpleInsertRow[]` (one row per package, location-level fields repeated, `pricing: 'full'` hardcoded, todaydate/updated_at omitted to fall through to Postgres defaults) and POSTs to `${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/pricing_simple` with `Prefer: return=representation` (atomic — all rows or none); on 2xx writes a `logSysadminAudit` entry with `action: "create_location"`, `target_type: "pricing_simple"`, `target_id: location_code`, `before: null`, `after: { location_code, location_pretty, package_count }` and returns `{ ok: true, location_code, package_count }`; new module-local `nonNegativeNumber` helper (accepts numbers or numeric strings, rejects NaN/Infinity/negatives). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — header comment lists 6 cards; `AddLocationCard` import added; new `AddLocationOperationCard` server component wraps the client island in the existing `OperationCard` shell. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — header comment extended; new `createLocationAction` reads location-level fields + iterates the 7 standard package indices reading `pkg_<i>_include`/`pkg_<i>_pkg`/`pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar`/`pkg_<i>_single`/`pkg_<i>_flash2`/`pkg_<i>_flash5`/`pkg_<i>_sort`, skips rows whose include checkbox isn't checked, builds the JSON body with literal `"pkg$"` key, POSTs via `sysadminPostJson("/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/create-location", body)`, surfaces `{ ok: true, message: "Location created: <code> (<count> packages)" }` or `{ ok: false, error }`; helper `STANDARD_PACKAGES` constant + `fieldNumber` + `fieldOptionalNullable` added. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 24 row marked Completed (2026-05-05); Briefs 25 + 26 added as Not started. `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` — brief-024 line commented out. `BUILD_STATE.md` — bumped Last updated; this entry; pages table sysadmin row updated to reflect 6 cards + new bundle size; prioritized work list rows added for 24/25/26. `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry extended to mention Brief 24's Add Location feature + the cross-worker cache-invalidation TODO. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AddLocationCard.tsx` — client island. `useState<boolean[]>` tracks per-row include state (default-on for the four standard-priced rows: bubble_bath/ultra_bath/bath/express; default-off for ext_exterior/extreme/works); the include checkbox's `onChange` flips the corresponding entry, which drives both `disabled={!isIncluded}` and `required={isIncluded}` on the per-row price inputs (mirrors the conditional-required pattern from UploadDocumentCard via Brief 20). Location-level fields (location_pretty, location_code with `pattern="[a-z0-9_]+"`, site, area_manager, regional_manager, site_email/am_email/rm_email) render as a 2-column grid. Packages render as a 7-row grid with desktop columns Include/Package/pkg$/single/flash2/flash5/sort; mobile collapses to 1 column. Default values pre-fill flash2 = "2.00" and flash5 = "5.00" on every row, sort 1-4 on the four standard-priced rows. Hidden `<input name="pkg_<i>_pkg">` carries the package name (read-only display). Submit wrapped in `<ActionForm action={createLocationAction}>` so the page-level UX matches the existing five cards (inline success/error, router.refresh on ok). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker uses raw `fetch` to Supabase REST**, not the supabase-js client, for the bulk insert. Brief explicitly specified the REST POST shape with `Prefer: return=representation` and the array body; raw fetch makes the literal `"pkg$"` JSON key unmistakable in code review. The audit log write below it still uses `createServiceClient(env)` for parity with the rest of the worker. (2) **Pre-check uniqueness on `location_code` only**, not the full composite (location_code, pkg) PK. The PK constraint catches inserts with duplicate (code, pkg) anyway; the pre-check exists so the UI can render a friendly 409 "Location code already in use" instead of letting Supabase return its own constraint-violation text. The race window between pre-check and insert is harmless (a second concurrent create with the same code 500s on PK violation; no data corruption possible because the array POST is atomic). (3) **`nonNegativeNumber` accepts numeric strings** (e.g., "12.50") in addition to JS numbers — the apps/web client island's `<input type="number">` actually submits a string in `FormData` even though it renders a number widget; the action coerces in `fieldNumber`, but defensive coercion at the worker boundary is cheap. (4) **`sort` validation requires positive integer** — the brief said "positive integer; if blank coerce to null"; non-integer floats reject with 400. (5) **Email regex is intentionally simple** (`/^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$/`); the brief allowed "or similar simple regex". Captures common typos without overreach (RFC 5322 is impractical; this is shape-only). (6) **Empty/whitespace optional fields coerce to null** via `stringOrNull` (worker) + `fieldOptionalNullable` (action) so the JSON body either includes a real value or omits the field entirely (the worker also accepts null for parity). (7) **`pkg$` literal column name preserved end-to-end:** TS interface `PricingSimpleInsertRow["pkg$"]`, JSON body literal `"pkg$"` key, FormData field name `pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` (sanitized for HTML — `$` is legal in form names but ESM modules and most HTML5 validators treat it cleanly while bracket-notation in JS handles it; using `pkg_dollar` in form names sidesteps any tooling that might choke on `$`). The action maps `pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` → `"pkg$"` exactly once at the JSON-body step. (8) **`pricing: 'full'` is hardcoded server-side**, never reads from body — operator has no way to create a location at any other initial mode (matches brief). (9) **`createLocationAction` returns the worker's `location_code` + `package_count` from the response body** (not just echoing the input); falls back to inputs if the worker's response shape is wrong. The success message is `"Location created: <code> (<count> packages)"`. (10) **Disabled price inputs on unchecked rows** rather than removing them from the DOM — keeps the column-aligned grid layout stable when toggling. The action treats `pkg_<i>_include = null` (unchecked) as "skip row entirely"; whether the disabled inputs submit a value is irrelevant. (11) **No client-side row count display** ("3 of 7 packages selected") — the worker already reports the count back in the success message; an inline counter would just duplicate. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-worker cache invalidation gap (TODO).** signup-worker caches `pricing_simple_resolved` for 5 minutes via `caches.default`; sysadmin-worker has no way to bust that cache today. New locations created via this brief surface on the customer signup form within 5 minutes (cache TTL) without operator intervention. The brief flagged this and listed "manual cache-clear button" as a Brief item — not added to INDEX.md as a numbered brief yet (operator decision); a code-comment TODO in `handleCreateLocation` points at the gap. (b) **Custom (non-standard) package names not supported in v1.** The 7 standard packages (bubble_bath/ultra_bath/bath/express/ext_exterior/extreme/works) are baked into AddLocationCard. Operator who needs an ad-hoc package can use a one-shot SQL or wait for Brief 25 (Update Package). The form's help text mentions this. (c) **Edit-after-create flow** is Brief 25's responsibility — Brief 24 is create-only. The card has no "Modify location" affordance. (d) **`getActiveLocationByCode`-equivalent for pricing_simple does NOT exist** in `@splash/db-supabase`'s `pricing.ts`; the worker's pre-check is inlined raw fetch. Adding a typed helper (`locationExistsByCode(client, code) -> boolean`) is a small follow-up and would also benefit Brief 25's search-by-location_code path. Not added in this brief to keep scope tight. (e) **No CSV/Excel bulk import** — out of scope per brief. (f) **No soft-delete / archive flow** — out of scope. (g) **`PricingSimpleRowWithRawPrices` in `packages/types/src/pricing.ts` carries 'NAME UNVERIFIED' caveats from the original port** that no longer apply — `pkg$/single/flash2/flash5` are now confirmed real columns by virtue of Brief 24's INSERT contract. A small docs cleanup brief could remove those caveats; not done here to keep the scope tight. (h) **`logSysadminAudit` accepts `actor` as a `{ id, email }` object** matching `AuthUser`-lite; the worker's `actor` local already has that shape from the top-of-`fetch` `auth.session` extraction, so passing it directly works (no adaptation needed). (i) **Bundle delta:** `/admin/sysadmin` 161 B → 3.93 kB / 105 kB → 109 kB First Load JS (+3.77 kB / +4 kB). Driven by AddLocationCard's React-state include toggles (7 rows × 1 useState slot + onChange handler each; the React state machinery itself is the dominant cost, not the markup). The brief's estimate of "+1-2 kB" was low; the seven independently-toggleable rows + the conditional `required`/`disabled` propagation pushed it higher. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 5.684s (10 cached + 3 fresh — apps/sysadmin-worker, apps/web, and the propagated turbo invalidations); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.9s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/sysadmin` 3.93 kB / 109 kB First Load JS (was 161 B / 105 kB pre-Brief-24 in this build snapshot). All other routes unchanged. | Claude Code (Brief 24 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 23 completed:** customer claim form ported to damage-worker — closes decision 9. **Files created:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` — exports `renderClaimForm`, `renderThanksPage`, `renderClaimNotFound`, and `htmlResponse`. Single-page port of the legacy two-section form (legacy multi-step JS, animated bubbles, drag-drop file zones, custom toggle switch, JS modals, and staff-password gate dropped per brief's "keep simple but correct" guidance). Splash-blue gradient header preserved, sourcing the logo via `ASSETS.logoWhite`. Inline `<script>` mirrors the equipment-malfunction checkbox into a hidden `equipmentMalfunction` input so the worker continues to read the legacy `"true"/"false"` string verbatim. EQUIPMENT_CHOICES + DETERMINATION_CHOICES inlined from legacy. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — route-table comment updated to list the two new GETs and the dual-mode POST; two new public-dispatch branches inserted between `/claims-api/photo/*` and `/manage/api/*`; `handleRenderClaimForm` + `handleRenderThanks` added after `dispatchManageApi`. Both validate the slug via `getActiveLocationByCode` and 404 with friendly HTML on miss. `handleClaimSubmission` made dual-mode: top-of-handler `browserMode = request.headers.get("Accept")?.includes("text/html")` flag drives `Response.redirect(..., 303)` on success (target `/claims/{slug}/thanks?id=<claimId>`) and on the catch branch (target `/claims/{slug}?error=<encoded>`). JSON callers (no `text/html` in Accept) continue to receive the existing JSON shape unchanged. Error-path slug recovery uses Referer header regex `/^\/claims\/([^/]+)/` because `request.formData()` is single-shot and may have already failed. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Field names match what `handleClaimSubmission` actually reads, not what the brief enumerated.** Brief listed `customerMailingAddress`, `damageDescription`, `preexistingDamage`, `staffNotes`, `submittedBy`; worker reads `mailingAddress`, `issueDescription`, `preExistingDamage`, `employeeName` (no `staffNotes` — built worker-side from `customerTold` + `customerDemeanor`). Brief explicitly told me the worker is source of truth ("Field names MUST match what handleClaimSubmission reads"); the brief's enumerated list was a recall miss. Form posts the legacy field names verbatim and hits the worker's existing parse pipeline cleanly. All 24 fields audit-matched against the worker's reads — zero drift. (2) **JSON ↔ 303 dual-mode keyed off `Accept` header**, not a `redirect=html` form field — Accept is what every browser sends automatically and JSON callers send `Accept: application/json`. Edge case: a programmatic caller with browser-shaped Accept gets redirected — flagged latent (no such caller in monorepo today). (3) **303 (See Other) over 302** for the POST→GET-on-success idiom — forces GET on the redirect target, dodges duplicate-submit-on-Back-button re-POST footgun. (4) **Thanks page is a separate `GET /claims/{slug}/thanks` route**, not inline POST response — bookmarkable URL with `?id=<claim-id>`, survives reload, dodges the back-button re-POST. (5) **Invalid slug renders 404 with friendly HTML** rather than 200 — differentiates "I made a typo" from "I'm logged in" for any external healthcheck the operator might wire up. HTML body is identical regardless. (6) **Required attrs match brief spec exactly:** customerName / customerPhone / vehicleMake / vehicleModel / issueDescription / determination / employeeName + the four photo inputs (legacy parity). Optional: vehicleColor / licensePlate / mailingAddress / customerTold / customerDemeanor / customerEmail / vehicleYear / membershipNumber / preExistingDamage. equipmentInvolved kept required for legacy parity (brief didn't enumerate it). (7) **`equipmentMalfunction` is a UI checkbox + hidden input** mirrored by a tiny inline `<script>`; worker reads the hidden `"true"/"false"` value. Only JS in the page. (8) **Visual port is simplified.** Splash-blue gradient header + white card + light-blue gradient page kept; bubbles / multi-step / drag-drop zones / JS modals dropped. Net code drop: ~700 lines of JS eliminated; ~250 lines HTML/CSS retained. (9) **Error-bounce slug recovery via Referer header** rather than `request.clone().formData()` — clone after consume throws, and the catch branch is most often hit precisely because `formData()` itself failed. Fallback slug `"unknown"` renders the not-found card; rare. (10) **URL-bounce caps:** `?error=` 240 chars, `?id=` 64 chars. Defends against malicious or malformed bounce content; `escHtml` defends against the rest. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`isOriginAllowed` not enforced on `POST /claims-api/submit-claim`** — public endpoint, intentional (legacy parity). Customer posts from arbitrary device-bookmarked URLs without referrer policy. CSRF risk negligible since no auth state to forge from cross-origin script. Worth noting for a future CSRF-posture brief. (b) **`getActiveLocationByCode` runs on every form GET, every thanks GET, and every POST submit** for `location_pretty` resolution. Three D1 reads per claim flow. Future optimization: cache active-locations table at module load. (c) **`location_pretty` posted by the form is not authoritative** — the worker re-resolves at POST time. Defense against DevTools edits is automatic. (d) **`equipmentMalfunction` parses `String === "true"`** — anything else is false. New form + legacy form emit lowercase; non-form callers should be aware. (e) **Programmatic caller with `text/html` in Accept would get 303 redirect** instead of JSON. No such caller today. Mitigation if it surfaces: `?json=1` query opt-out. Out of scope. (f) **Browser-mode error path caps message at 240 chars.** Realistic worker errors are short; cap is a safety bound. (g) **No 1MB / 100MB upload-size guard at form level** — legacy didn't have one either; CF Workers caps at 100 MB. Customers exceeding that see a CF error page, not the form's banner. **`handleClaimSubmission` response reconciliation (Report q1):** browser submit → 303 redirect; programmatic JSON caller → existing JSON shape. **Where renderDamageForm landed (Report q2):** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` (extracted module — too large to inline cleanly even after simplification). **Field-name audit (Report q3):** all 24 form fields match the worker's reads at index.ts:1117-1146 (full table in the brief Outcome). **Bundle-size delta (Report q4):** post-Brief-23 damage-worker bundle is **1628.16 KiB / 368.53 KiB gzip**. No clean pre-Brief baseline was captured for an exact delta — the brief's 10-30 KiB gzip estimate is consistent with the observed payload. Comfortably under CF's 3 MiB compressed free-tier limit. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 2.707s (12 cached + 1 fresh — only `@splash/damage-worker` invalidated). `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded. Bindings inventoried: DB / R2_BUCKET / IMAGES. Staging routes for `staging.splashcarwashes.info/claims/*` were already wired in Brief 16 — no wrangler.toml change. | Claude Code (Brief 23 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 22 completed:** recent-notes sub-box added inside `/admin/damage/[id]`'s SummaryCard with a jump-to-add-note anchor button. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — `SummaryCard` props extended with `activity: ClaimActivityRow[]`; new server-rendered `RecentNotesBox` sub-component renders after `ApprovalDetails` using the same `mt-4 rounded-splash-md border border-gray-light bg-sudsy-blue-soft/30 p-4` idiom. Box header carries the "Recent notes" label on the left and a small `<a href="#add-note">Add note</a>` styled as a white-bg + splash-blue-text + splash-blue-bordered button (mirrors the no-access-card "Back to Dashboard" idiom from /admin/sysadmin). Empty state: `<p className="text-sm italic text-splash-navy/60">No notes yet.</p>`. Populated state: up to 3 notes (filtered by `activity_type === "note"`, sorted desc by `created_at` with id-desc tie-break, sliced to 3) rendered as a `<ul>` of `<li>` items — timestamp (mono `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm` via existing `formatDateTime`), actor name (bold), note text (`whitespace-pre-line`). When notes exist, a "View all activity ↓" link anchors to `#activity` at the bottom-right. `AddNoteCard` wrapper `<div>` gains `id="add-note"`. `ActivityTimelineCard` wrapper `<div>` gains `id="activity"`. Top-of-file section comment updated: section 3 (SummaryCard appends Recent notes), section 7 (timeline carries `id="activity"`), section 8 (add-note carries `id="add-note"`). `apps/web/app/globals.css` — added `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }` with a Brief 22 comment. Affects every anchor jump site-wide; harmless on routes without anchors. **Files created:** none. RecentNotesBox is implemented inline as a server component within `page.tsx` (single-use, mirroring `ApprovalDetails`/`AuditStamps` siblings). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Placement: inside the SummaryCard at the bottom**, after ApprovalDetails — best serves the brief's "alongside the claim summary" goal and reuses the existing sub-box visual idiom rather than introducing a third stacked card above the Move-forward section. (2) **"Add note" button styling: white-bg + splash-blue text + splash-blue border** — secondary affordance treatment that doesn't compete with the primary splash-blue-fill submit buttons in the transitions section or the AddNoteCard's own submit at the bottom. (3) **"View all activity ↓" anchor link added** as the optional brief feature; renders only when notes exist (no point pointing at an empty timeline). (4) **Sort defensively even though the worker SELECTs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC** — `ActivityTimelineCard` itself defensively re-sorts (page.tsx:1162-1166), so RecentNotesBox matches that posture rather than depending on a fetch-order invariant the worker layer could change without the UI layer noticing. (5) **`whitespace-pre-line` on note text** — matches the timeline's own note rendering; preserves multi-line notes typed into the textarea. (6) **`scroll-behavior: smooth` applied globally on `html`** rather than scoped to a class on the anchor target. The CSS property only takes effect on the scrolling element (typically `html` or `body`); putting it on the target itself has no effect. The brief offered the scoped fallback but it wouldn't actually work as written. Global is the simplest correct path; the app uses very few anchor jumps so side effects are minimal. (7) **`id="activity"`** added even though only the optional "View all activity ↓" link consumes it today — tiny markup; future briefs can deep-link into the timeline without re-touching this file. (8) **No client island** — notes list, jump button, and timeline-anchor link are all server-rendered HTML/CSS; browser-native smooth scroll + plain `<a href="#…">` anchors avoid any "use client" need. Bundle delta is zero. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Empty-state UX placement.** When the claim has zero notes, the box still renders the title + "Add note" button alongside the "No notes yet." line. The brief's empty-state spec was interpreted as "instead of an empty bulleted list", not "instead of the entire box including the jump button" — the jump button is more useful when the claim has zero notes than when it has many. (b) **`View all activity ↓` link in populated state only.** With zero notes the link is hidden — but the operator can still scroll, and the empty-notes case already implies a sparse claim. (c) **Notes list cap is 3, hardcoded.** Future briefs that want "5 most recent" or a per-user pref can lift the constant. (d) **`formatDateTime` slice trick** preserves the worker's DB-side ISO without timezone shift; works for both "YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss" (D1) and "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss(.sss)Z" (Postgres) shapes since both have HH:mm at offsets 11-15. (e) **Smooth-scroll global rule** affects every anchor jump site-wide. Only this brief's two anchors (#add-note, #activity) consume it today. If the app ever ships a feature where instant jump is preferable, wrap that surface in a container with `scroll-behavior: auto` to override. (f) **Bundle size unchanged at 3.08 kB / 108 kB First Load JS** for `/admin/damage/[id]` — RecentNotesBox is server-only markup, the anchor-link CSS is plain Tailwind, and the smooth-scroll rule is in globals.css (already part of the layout).
